# Revision log — OWNED editorial revision of 14 September 2026

This log records how each recommendation in `REVIEW.md` (the collection review) and the per-post `REVIEW.md` files was handled. The review files are unchanged and remain the baseline. "Implemented" means the recommendation was applied as written; "Adapted" means the intent was met a different way; "Declined" means it was not applied, with the reason; "Unresolved" means work or verification that could not be completed in this pass.

## Collection-level decisions

**Structure (collection review §"Reading order, combinations and worthwhile additions")**

- Split the handover material out of "The Roadmap Did Not Slip, the Financing Did" → **Implemented.** New Part V chapter `31-handover-of-obligations` ("Hand Over the Obligations, Not Just the Company", permalink `handover-of-obligations`). Chapter 18 keeps its permalink and title and is now a focused delayed-financing chapter with a completed, dated funding decision.
- Move "Fix the Decision Problem Before Adding People" directly after "Can the Software and the Team Deliver What Was Promised?" → **Implemented** in `config.yaml`; Part III introduction groups the nine chapters into three moves; handoffs rewritten (09→13→27→10).
- Optional fund-economics reference for waterfalls and fund ratios → **Implemented.** New reference page `fund-economics` ("Fund Economics: Fees, Distributions and Performance Reports") receives the €160 waterfall from chapter 2 and DPI/RVPI/TVPI material from chapter 4; both chapters link to it as optional depth.
- Combine "Find the Help" and "Useful Engagement" for a shorter edition → **Declined.** Kept separate with sharpened jobs (sourcing decision vs filled charter and scope change); repeated provider/charter advice removed instead.
- Keep Hilton/Skype paired; keep Visma and TeamSystem separate; keep acquisition and separation in one chapter with visible substructure → **Implemented.**
- Add a documented venture funding delay / minority case and a corporate ownership case → **Declined for this pass.** No documented case with adequate evidence was available without inventing facts; the evidence-scope disclosure is preserved in the guide, Part VI introduction and bibliography. Recorded as an open item.
- Companion evidence essay for healthcare/research methods → **Declined.** The finale keeps the material findings and shortens the methodological exposition instead.
- Three short reading routes in the guide → **Implemented** (plus the previous situation routes, consolidated into one table).
- Complete finding-to-funded-plan-to-review example → **Implemented** as a shared fictional chain (finding D-3, initiative ONB-1) through chapters 22–25 (diligence, first hundred days, financing slipped, handover) and the toolkit; figures and assumptions are stated in each chapter and labelled fictional.

**Titles (collection review §3)** — permalinks and ids unchanged: "Match the Funding to the Work" (raise-what-you-need); "Find the Cash Behind Your Technology Budget" (obligations-before-budget); "Toys R Us: Positive Operating Earnings, Too Little Cash" (toys-r-us); "TeamSystem: Each New Owner Inherits Progress and Unfinished Work" (teamsystem). Visma's title decision is recorded in its entry.

**Terminology (collection review §"Voice, readability and terminology")** — a terminology sheet (investor/firm/fund/company; shareholder vs accountable leader; financial sponsor vs investor-side sponsor vs company sponsor; funding states from expression of interest to cash received; named profit measures; financial vs causal contribution; end-to-end work lead time vs DORA change lead time; continuing service as a legitimate outcome; thesis and durable value) governed every chapter revision and the glossary.

**Categorical ownership contrasts (collection review §1)** — the "companies without investors do X; under investors Y" pattern was removed from every opening, summary and comic introduction listed in the review and replaced by the conditional pattern ("this arrangement changes the conditions of the decision; establish the actual funding, authority and deadline before committing", then the specific condition).

**Companion formats** — summaries were rewritten to carry the revised argument and checked against a 300–500-word prose convention (front matter absent; figure lines and `[S..]` citation tokens excluded). Comic text (introductions, captions, dialogue, JSON prompt/alt/caption fields) was edited; no panel images could be regenerated in this pass (no image-generation key available). Panels whose new text the existing image cannot fully carry are flagged `needs_regeneration` in their JSON and listed per post below.

**Not changed** — `REVIEW.md` files; permalinks; ids; image files; `_research/` provenance (except a new `bibliography-revision-history.md` receiving the editorial chronology moved out of the public bibliography).

## 00-customers-lenders-investors — Customers, Lenders and Investors: What Each Expects in Return (`customers-lenders-investors`)

**Files changed:** index.md, summary.md, comics.md, spec.md
**Implemented:**
- Complete the opening example (high) → New "Larkspur Needs €100,000" section names the company and cast; new "The Same €100,000, Three Ways" table shows customer prepayment vs bank loan vs new shares with one obligation per route that changes the delivery promise; decision paragraph gives chosen option (prepayment), rejected alternatives (loan, share issue) with reasons, funding, scarce capacity (8 engineer-weeks of a four-person team), authority (board approves multi-year customer contracts; Ines signs) and the evidence that would reopen it (fewer than three signatures by 15 January, or estimate above 12 engineer-weeks). Every figure labelled fictional. Summary and comic panels 1 and 6 carry the same decision.
- Remove the "amount is the less important half" ranking (high) → Replaced in article opening, KEY POINTS, summary opening and comic introduction with the review's suggested "a budget tells you how much; the funding terms tell you when, which approvals and what obligations" formulation, plus "affordability depends on both".
- Shorten "Public and Private Ownership" (medium) → Section cut to three paragraphs; IPO paragraph and audit-frequency/reporting-category detail removed and replaced by a link to [[glossary]]; secondary-market cash distinction and one reporting sentence with S55 kept; S54 kept in the shares section.
- Investor labels as orientation only (medium) → Five paragraphs collapsed into one four-row table (label / typical arrangement / what to check) titled "Investor Labels Are Orientation, Not Terms", citations S58/S59/S60/S01 kept in one line, consequences handed to [[raise-what-you-need]].
- Curate the ending (low) → Questions cut from five to four; reading list cut from five to four with "Start here" (Brealey) and "Go deeper" (Myers) labels; capital-structure note moved from the shares section to a short note after the funding comparison, as the review suggested.
- Verify Myers and Robb–Robinson annotations (evidence) → Both fetched 2026-09-14. Myers 2001 (AEA page): abstract states "there is no universal theory of the debt-equity choice" and reviews tradeoff, pecking-order and free-cash-flow theories as conditional; annotation narrowed accordingly. Robb–Robinson (NBER w16272 page): Kauffman Firm Survey, US firms in their first year; finding is heavy reliance on external debt such as bank financing (often on the founder's personal balance sheet) and less on friends-and-family funding; annotation narrowed to that population and finding, dropping "mostly on bank debt rather than equity".
**Adapted:**
- "Move audit frequency and the fuller IPO discussion to the glossary" → Removed from this chapter and linked to [[glossary]]; the glossary already defines IPO but has no audit entry, and I may not edit the glossary. Coordinator note below.
- Reading list → Dropped the OECD scoreboard entry (its annotation was the weakest link to the chapter's argument) rather than keeping five entries; the remaining four are within source scope.
**Declined:**
- None.
**Unresolved / needs separate action:**
- Glossary: add a short "audited financial statements" entry (and, optionally, a note on public-reporting categories) so the chapter's [[glossary]] link lands on the promised detail.
- Figures 1 and 2 unchanged and still valid. No comic panel needs regeneration; panels 1 and 6 captions/prompts/alt rewritten within what the existing images show.
- The Larkspur €100,000 figures (6% loan ≈ €3,000/month over 36 months; 111 new shares ≈ 10% of 1,111) do not reconcile with other chapters' Larkspur scenarios, per the brief's instruction that each example states its own assumptions.
**Verification:** arithmetic rechecked (€100,000 at 6% over 36 months ≈ €3,042/month; 111/1,111 = 9.99%; 10/1,000 = 1%); all [[…]] targets resolve to existing permalinks; comic JSON parses, six panels, status unchanged; summary word count 499; article prose 1,599 words → timetoread 8 min; build not run.


## 01-announcement-is-not-a-budget — An Investment Announcement Is Not a Budget (`announcement-is-not-a-budget`)

**Files changed:** index.md, summary.md, comics.md, spec.md
**Implemented:**
- Correct the founder-owned/earnings-to-budget opening across formats (high) → Article second paragraph, summary opening and comic introduction now use the review's suggested formulation ("An announcement describes a transaction. To turn it into a hiring plan, establish how much cash reaches the business, when it arrives and who can authorize its use. These questions matter under every ownership arrangement") followed by the brief's conditional pattern ("a transaction changes the conditions of the answer…"). The "in a company with no outside investor…" sentence is gone from all three formats.
- Make one completed funding map the payoff (high) → New section "Alex's Hire: The Completed Funding-and-Authority Record": seven-row table (proposed commitment, paying entity, cash state, approver, conditions, runway effect, decision date) with a column naming the information source (Alex / finance: subscription agreement, bank statement, closing statement, cash plan / board minutes / legal: shareholders' agreement consent schedule). Decision paragraph gives the chosen option (three hires authorized by Ines on 12 October within the board-approved plan), alternatives rejected (five hires — above plan, needs investor director consent; the "innovation budget" — exists in no agreement), funding (€7.6m net received vs €8m announced), scarce capacity (recruiting time, team absorption), authority (CEO within plan; board/investor director for changes) and evidence that would reopen it. One paragraph shows how the record changes under the €40m purchase of existing shares. Summary and comic panel 6 carry the same record.
- Move the detailed waterfall to an optional fund-economics note (medium) → "How Fees and Profit Sharing Work" replaced by "Fees and Profit Sharing Shape Incentives": management fee, carry and waterfall named in one paragraph, with the incentive point and links to [[different-bets]], [[three-different-returns]] and [[fund-economics]]; the €160 table, preferred return, catch-up and clawback definitions moved verbatim to fund-economics; the SEC conflicts paragraph (S01) kept here. Summary's waterfall arithmetic replaced by the same brief statement and links. Metrick–Yasuda annotation adjusted to point at the fund-economics reference.
- Bring the entity diagram forward and label the sale route (medium) → Mermaid map moved from the end of the chapter to directly after the entity table; buyer arrow relabelled "Payment for the portfolio company's shares"; new paragraph states that in this diagram the buyer purchases the portfolio company's shares from the holding vehicle (payment lands in the holding vehicle, repays lenders, remainder to the fund) and that in other transactions the buyer purchases the holding vehicle from the fund; either way it is payment for existing shares, not new company money. - Qualify the GP/manager sentence (clarity) → Now: "In the common partnership form, the general partner… holds the management responsibilities and powers under the fund arrangements. The investment firm often acts as the fund's manager under a separate agreement, and 'GP' is used informally for the firm; which body actually approves an investment is whatever those agreements say." Matches the glossary's GP entry. Summary aligned.

- High: give the inherited-arrangement reader an action → "Who is authorized" paragraph: Alex and Priya bring the revised scope, the €610,000 requirement with components, the alternative (two implementation specialists, ~€300,000 a year recurring) and the review date; Ines and Sam negotiate and bring terms to the board; the shareholders' agreement governs shareholder consent. Key point 3, the summary and comic panel 6 carry the same split.
- Medium: develop one Larkspur case with two feasible arrangements and a choice → Arrangement A (€2.5m growth equity with approval rights and an 18-month second-country expectation) vs Arrangement B (€750,000 three-year term loan at 8%, staged work); B chosen under stated assumptions (€350,000 operating cash, customer evidence for the setup constraint, no second-country evidence); example ends with chosen option, alternatives rejected, funding, scarce capacity, who is authorized and the evidence that would reverse it (signed second-country customers; cohort effort still above 70 hours).

## glossary — Glossary (`glossary`)

# Revision log — OWNED editorial revision of 15 September 2026

This pass implements the reassessed reviews dated 14–15 September 2026 (collection `REVIEW.md` and the 41 per-post `REVIEW.md` files, which are unchanged and remain the independent baseline). It was coordinated as one pass with one agent per post, run in dependency order: standalone chapters first, then the shared-scenario clusters after a canonical record was fixed, then the reading guide, Part V introduction, toolkit and bibliography. Every per-post entry below is the agent's own report of what changed, what was adapted or declined, what stays unresolved and what was verified; the coordinator checked cross-links, summary lengths, comic metadata, the build and the shared figures across posts.

## Collection-level decisions

- **One canonical shared-scenario record.** Two read-only inventories of every passage carrying the shared Larkspur examples showed the chain could not be reconciled sentence by sentence, so the coordinator fixed one dated ledger before any of those chapters was edited. It is kept in `_journals/private-techuity/_research/shared-scenario-record.md` (outside the site build). The decisions that changed text: the first-hundred-days envelope is **€500,000 and 24 engineer-weeks** (the allocation chapter's figure), so the allocation, early-plan, recovery, toolkit and handover chapters now describe one plan; KNW-1 is four protected specialist-weeks outside the 24; portal research (€20,000 / 2 weeks) is a funded plan line; the REC-1 correction and retest cost **€20,000 / 2 weeks from the reserve, approved by the board around day 47**; the day-100 data-quality step uses the last four weeks and €40,000, leaving **€160,000 and no uncommitted weeks**; ONB-1 is **€180,000 committed, about €90,000 incurred by day 100, €90,000 payable over the next two quarters**, with €30,000/yr maintenance from the operating budget from the next financial year; the day-20 baseline is reconstructed from the twelve implementations of the previous two quarters; Priya measures the cohort at day 90 and the board decides at day 100 under one pre-set rule (**< 60 h green, 60–70 h amber, > 70 h or no reduction red**; 62 h is amber); gate 2 at month six is ≤ 50 h with waiting time down; the expansion decision moves to the next quarterly review (≈ day 190) and expansion is not before month 13; the delegation rule is that Ines authorizes spending inside the approved plan while reserve draws, changes to what the envelope funds and hires outside the headcount plan are the board's.
- **Pilot cost basis and the funding-choice loan.** €75/h fully loaded, 2,000 hours per FTE-year, so an implementation specialist costs €150,000 a year and two cost €300,000; the pilot is €180,000 build plus €30,000/yr maintenance everywhere (the obligations chapter's €200,000 aligned); released staff time is capacity until a dated conversion is evidenced. The funding-choice loan is a **four-year** €750,000 term loan at 8% with a one-year principal holiday (payments €60,000 / €310,000 / €290,000 / €270,000, closing at zero) tested against €350,000 of operating cash alone.
- **Finale cost split.** The €220,000 is €150,000 of specialist payroll (cash, ending only when protected customer contracts allow) plus €70,000 of released support time (capacity); Option A as written is inadmissible under the scenario's own contract rule; Option B saves €80,000 of cash from year two after ≈€75,000 of one-off specialist retention.
- **The financing-delay chapter is a separate scenario.** It keeps its own cash, burn, hires and dates, no longer uses the shared identifiers or ledger figures, and says so; the reading guide, Part V introduction, handover chapter and toolkit describe the chain as diligence → early plan → handover, with earlier pilot and support episodes in Parts III–IV.
- **Investor callouts.** Kept where they name a financing or governance consequence, shortened or reworded where they restated the learning outcome or reintroduced a universal claim; "the investor reports to its investors" became a fund manager reporting to its fund's investors where that is the actor.
- **Confirmed factual corrections.** Unicorn study: reported valuations average about 50% above modelled fair value (NBER version; the published JFE version says 48%). Fund catch-up: a slower catch-up changes timing, not necessarily the final split. TeamSystem: the €72.0m net finance charge is an accounting figure; €52.1m of finance costs were paid (Note 10), and the 2024 £34.189m gross realization proceeds are reported separately from the £24.3m July announcement, neither a net distribution. Hilton's OnQ is dated to 2003/2005 pre-buyout filings; Skype's Qik acquisition and group video calling to January 2011 (S-1/A).
- **Summaries.** All 30 are within 466–500 words by the collection's counter after the pass (the coordinator trimmed the adviser and financing-delay summaries by a few words at the end).
- **Rate-limit interruptions.** Two waves of agents were terminated mid-task by API session limits; the coordinator relaunched each post with an instruction to inspect the diff and complete the work, and the per-post entries describe the total change against the manuscript at `ab7a8f4`. The author's intermediate commit `4bd1d51` ("private techuity first draft", 12:32) captured part of the work in progress, including a site build; later agents verified that work against the canonical record rather than redoing it.

Closing paragraph separates the rate during the catch-up from the final entitlement; only no catch-up or a catch-up that never completes changes the final split (worked: sale at €109 → LPs €108, GP €1 = 11%). KEY POINTS bullet 1: "A slower catch-up changes when the manager is paid, not necessarily how much."

## Review response, 16 September 2026 (five findings)

- **1. Customer commitments (32):** the second customer, which does not agree to a date beyond its contract, is sequenced first so its configuration goes live by 30 June inside the contractual date; the first customer accepts September by agreement before the notices; the termination right is kept as a recorded risk and no revenue is counted before go-live. TL;DR and storyboard aligned.
- **2. Hiring approval (13):** the decision now carries the reserved matter’s full scope (hiring, dismissal and pay of executive officers, including any change to Priya’s role or pay); the senior product manager fills one of the five board-approved second-team roles, so Ines appoints within the headcount plan, with the rule stated that cash alone does not establish hiring authority; a CPO is recorded as outside the plan (board approval of role and budget plus consent to hiring and pay). Spec and TL;DR aligned.
- **3. Cash-timing conclusion (32):** made conditional on Larkspur’s notice periods, severance terms and dates, with the general lesson stated as comparing transition costs, savings and funding by date; key points, TL;DR, comic panel 3 and the Figure 1 prompt and caption carry the qualification.
- **4. Knowledge-transfer fallback (32):** the two departing second-country engineers are the pair on three months’ notice (employed to 31 January); test in the second week of December, retest 15 January; a fixed-term extension of up to two months from 1 February for one of them, by agreement recorded before the notices, is funded as a contingency of up to €17,000 held in the plan; it ends 31 March and serves the transfer or, failing that, the second customer’s configuration; if declined, contractor time under the remaining engineer; re-planning at the 1 April review. Base cash unchanged (€505,000 at 30 June); contingency case €488,000, reserve date moved from the second to the first week of August. Reviews and reversing evidence updated.
- **Author request (32):** opening paragraph added on outside investment cutting both ways for a team (headcount can grow significantly and quickly; the obligations and pressures that come with the money can force cost reduction just as quickly, which is how unpopular and damaging layoffs arise); TL;DR opening aligned, spec criterion and changelog added.
- **Second review response (32), four remaining findings.** The first response left contradictions, recorded here rather than marked resolved:
  - *Customer commitment.* Sequencing the second customer to June had treated the 30 June termination right as agreement to June delivery; the contracts promise first-quarter go-live. Now: the first customer signs an amendment (go-live and termination deadline to 30 September, waiver as before) before the notices; the second customer declines any change and its 31 March promise is kept by retaining one second-country specialist on an agreed fixed term to 31 March (€17,000, now in the plan rather than a contingency), with the engineer-month arithmetic stated (about eleven engineer-months for rules and the standard configuration, five for the bespoke one; sixteen available) and the termination right recorded as the remaining risk.
  - *Transfer fallback.* The extension had ended before the work it was said to protect. Now the specialist works the real second-country work to 31 March; the transfer test runs in the first week of March on that work (a rule change and the second customer’s configuration released by the remaining engineer alone) and is read on 15 March; the only fallback is one further month to 30 April (€8,500, by the same agreement), retest read on 15 April; if it fails again the board re-plans on 1 May with three recorded options; no contractor is assumed. Departure dates, savings (€63,500 in February and March, €72,000 from April), one-off timing (€13,000 in March) and cash recomputed: €488,000 at 30 June is now the base case, reserve in the first week of August; the contingency case is about €480,000.
  - *Employment law.* “At least a third” corrected to the statute’s 33 percent and the 4,000-hour employer test qualified as exclusive of overtime; the passage split into four paragraphs (153, 87, 163, 140 words) with the detail retained in S77 and S78; consultation and notification kept distinct; Larkspur’s notice, severance and retention terms labelled fictional.
  - *Reviews.* Re-dated so each reads evidence that exists: 1 December (notices, bridge, amendment, second-customer progress), 1 February (readiness), 1 March (February’s actual savings), 15 March (transfer test), 1 April (second customer live, March savings, round stage), 1 May (April’s first full-month savings and the contingency outcome). Alternatives table, key sentence on first full month, TL;DR and comic panel 5 aligned.
- **5. Employment law (32, bibliography, glossary):** thresholds restated with the directive’s boundaries (more than 20 and fewer than 100; at least 100 and fewer than 300; 300 or more; or 20 over 90 days at the member state’s choice), the definition, the five-redundancy assimilation rule, consultation distinguished from written notification and the 30-day period, member states’ freedom to be more favourable; the US statute’s employer definition (including the aggregate-hours alternative and part-time exclusion), single-site 30-day tests, 90-day aggregation, notice recipients, reduced-notice exceptions and state law noted; Larkspur’s notice and severance terms labelled fictional contract terms. S77 and S78 consulted scopes rewritten after re-reading both statutes; glossary row for collective consultation corrected.

## 00-customers-lenders-investors — Customers, Lenders and Investors: What Each Expects in Return (`customers-lenders-investors`)

**Files changed:** index.md, summary.md, comics.md, spec.md, `assets/images/00-customers-lenders-investors/cash-arrives-with-obligations.jpeg` (regenerated), `assets/images/00-customers-lenders-investors/comic-06-scene.jpeg` (regenerated; previous version kept in `_research/discarded-comic-variants/`), `_research/article-illustration-prompts-20260913.json`, `_research/comic-generation-prompts-20260913.json`, `_research/comic-generation-progress-20260913.json`, `docs/private-techuity/customers-lenders-investors.html`, `.spec.html` and the two built assets.
**Implemented:**
- CLI-001 (high) — fallback sequence → customers sign orders in early January that bind Larkspur only when it countersigns; 15 January is the count-and-estimate checkpoint; Ines countersigns on 16 January only with three orders and an estimate of twelve engineer-weeks or fewer; with two orders she countersigns only after the board has approved the €35,000 loan; with one or none, no order is countersigned and the customer is offered a revised, smaller order. Each case states which contracts bind, when cash arrives, which date is promised and whose agreement a change needs. Summary and comic panel 6 carry "nothing is promised until the CEO countersigns".
- CLI-002 (medium) — cash-flow assumptions → new "Where the €100,000 Goes" subsection: engineers' salaries already paid from subscriptions; new cash = €30,000 licence and hosting + €25,000 backfill contract engineer + €35,000 implementation contractors + €10,000 reserve; two engineers full time from Monday 19 January finish eight engineer-weeks by 13 February (twelve would end 27 February with no margin); cash in the bank by 30 January, contractors ordered from 1 February; the one-month refund (≈€8,300) is paid from the reserve, otherwise from about six months of the €1,500 surplus, stated as the accepted risk; the €35,000 loan's first payment falls at the end of February and comes from the surplus.
- CLI-003 (medium) — loan terms and the €35,000 → "fixed 6% a year", "36 equal payments"; €33,333 gap rounded up to a €35,000 loan (≈€1,065/month, ≈€435 headroom); the surplus could carry at most ≈€49,000; one-customer gap of €66,667 needs ≈€2,030/month. Summary no longer calls €35,000 the maximum.
- CLI-004 (medium) — consent right scope → table and rejection reason now say the investor can block spending above €50,000 outside the approved plan and, from the board seat, judges the roadmap; "second approver" removed; comic caption says "a board seat and a say over large unplanned spending".
- CLI-005 (medium) — discount case → 10% discount yields €90,000 and a €10,000 gap; nominal cost (€10,000 vs ≈€9,500 interest) kept separate from affordability, which the loan still fails.
- CLI-006 (medium) — terminology → callout reworded without "equity investors", "instrument", "prices those terms"; CEO/CFO/CTO expanded with responsibilities; bond sentence in plain words; SEC expanded; minority stake, funding round, buyout, "loan repayments" (for "service borrowing"), "part of the parent's business group", articles ("the company's rulebook"), "the permission the board has given her" (for delegation), engineer-week, consent right, board of directors, dispatch feature, onboarding, prepayment, founder, investment fund, listed, IPO and audited statements all glossed inline at first use; "reforecast" → "reworks the plan"; personal guarantee and working paper explained in the reading list.
- CLI-007 (medium) — standalone formats → summary defines principal, interest, equity, prepayment, the board, the consent right and the four labels; comic intro explains investment fund; panel 1 explains grants, panel 4 explains share price and reported profit versus cash, panel 5 explains the rulebook and shareholder agreements the artwork shows; panel 6 caption rewritten as short steps and the panel regenerated with the journal comic generator (three offer cards: customer contract with delivery calendar, bank loan with repayment calendar, share certificate with an empty board chair; Sam holding the budget sheet; dialogue unchanged). Image inspected: matches the caption; the speech bubble has three tails, a minor drawing quirk left as is.
- CLI-008 (medium) — structure → opening prose cut to two paragraphs; Larkspur, the cast and the assumptions moved directly after; public/private ownership and the investor-label table moved after the completed decision; the decision split into "The choice / Who decides / Why not the loan / Why not the shares" and the fallback into a four-case list; summary's example split into three short paragraphs.
- CLI-009 (low) — Figure 1 → regenerated with the article illustrator (prompt requires single-headed arrows, money in, service/repayment/certificate/asset out; "Investor" label renamed "Shareholder" as the audit suggested); image inspected at full size and at 390 px: cash points into the company, the sold asset points to the buyer. Prompt archive updated with the new prompt and checksum.
**Adapted:** the review's suggestion to "remove the category detour" from the comic was met by regenerating panel 6 around the three offers rather than dropping the labels from the article, where the spec still requires the orientation table. Summary trimmed to 498 words (figure line and citation token excluded) to stay within the 300–500 convention after the added definitions. timetoread 10 → 14 min (2,736 → 3,658 words by the same count that gave the previous label).

The summary overview image's "Operating company" and "share certificate" labels were left; the summary text now explains the recipient and share-issue ideas around it.
**Unresolved / needs separate action:** none for this article. `_research/artwork-review-20260913.json` still lists the superseded checksums for the two regenerated images; it is a dated acceptance record and was not rewritten.

Both comic generators pass their dry-run assertions (caption in prompt, panel/caption text present, dialogue pattern); the illustrator placeholder validated; `python3 _wiring/build.py` printed `[built] private-techuity`; the built page carries the three modalities, no unresolved `[[…]]` links, the spec link and all ten image assets, with the two regenerated files byte-identical to their sources; headless Chrome screenshots at 1280 px and 390 px of the article, TL;DR and comic tabs inspected (tabs switch by hash, tables and figures fit the narrow width). # Revision log — in-depth review of An Investment Announcement Is Not a Budget, 17 September 2026

Response to round 1 of the in-depth review (`.in-depth-reviews/20260917-115853.UVGj1y/round-01.review.md`, findings AIB-001 to AIB-008 plus a terminology audit). The spec was revised before the article; permalink and title unchanged.

## 01-announcement-is-not-a-budget — An Investment Announcement Is Not a Budget (`announcement-is-not-a-budget`)

**Files changed:** index.md, summary.md, comics.md, spec.md, `assets/images/01-announcement-is-not-a-budget/four-separate-organizations.jpeg` (regenerated), `assets/images/01-announcement-is-not-a-budget/capital-commitment-to-company-cash.jpeg` (regenerated), `_research/article-illustration-prompts-20260913.json` (both entries re-keyed to the current slug, with the previous prompt and checksum retained), `docs/private-techuity/announcement-is-not-a-budget.html`, `.spec.html` and the two built copies of the figures.
**Implemented:**
- AIB-001 (medium) — overbroad funding claim → opening and summary now say: of the money paid for shares, payment for new shares goes to the issuing company and payment for existing shares goes to the selling shareholder; other parts may repay loans or pay transaction costs; company cash can also grow when a lender makes the company a loan as part of the arrangement or an owner separately agrees to fund it. "Only the primary part … increases the company's cash" and the summary's "only money paid for newly issued shares reaches the company" are gone. Payment at closing is stated as the assumption of this chapter's examples, not a universal timetable.
- AIB-002 (medium) — terminology → defined at first use in the article: budget, valuation, share/shareholder, transaction costs, closing, transaction, prepayment (recap), minority funding round, buyout, corporate group, onboarding engineers, €8 million (€8m), venture and growth investors, entity, fund (a pool of investors' money managed under agreed rules; "investment vehicle" and "committed capital" removed), proceeds, financial sponsor, limited partners, capital call, equity, distribution (all before the diagram), lenders' contractual protections, "has already placed all the money it may invest" (for fully invested), €2 billion, debt, balance sheet (owns / owes / left for owners), cloud migration, pension funds and insurers, capital, partnership and limited partnership agreement, private funds, private-equity fund, exit, management fee (with its calculation base), carried interest "also called carry", investment profits, "the detailed rules for sharing investment proceeds … the distribution waterfall", realization in words, shared-cost splitting and services from connected organizations, the US Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) expanded, subscription agreement, issuer in words, chief executive officer (CEO), closing statement, shareholders' agreement, board minutes, reserved matters, investor director, runway, net spending, cash plan, onboarding volume, "cash it expects to generate from running the business", "an investor with plenty of money and a company short of cash", purchase price (for transaction consideration), "interest and repayment instalments" (for service it), public corporation and patient capital in the reading list. Preferred return, catch-up and clawback are no longer named in the chapter (they stay in [[fund-economics]]). Mermaid edges relabelled in plain words ("Promised money, paid when called", "Buys ownership", "Loan money under agreed terms", "Owns", "Payments to its owner that the agreements permit", "Sale proceeds", "Distributions to investors under the agreed sharing rules") and the LP and portfolio-company nodes carry their descriptions. Summary defines valuation, shares, closing, minority round, buyout, holding vehicle, LP, commitment, capital call, GP, carried interest, SEC, investor director and "chief executive". Comic intro defines valuation, closing, investment fund and names Ines as chief executive officer (CEO); panel captions explain shares, holding company, entities, "nothing moves until the investor pays", new versus existing shares and the fee/carry recipients; panel 6 explains the VENTURE / GROWTH / BUYOUT / CORPORATE cards.
- AIB-003 (medium) — Figure 2 → regenerated with the article illustrator: five stations (Commitment with one dashed "Promise" token; Capital-call notice; Investor pays into Fund cash; Approval gate; then two branches, New shares → Company cash and Existing shares → Seller). The unexplained "P" tokens are gone; the notice and the payment are separate steps; the seller branch shows that an approved investment need not increase company cash. Alt text and caption carry the same qualifications. Inspected at full size: all labels correct, no amounts.
- AIB-004 (low) — Figure 1 debt arrow → regenerated: a teal "Loan cash" arrow from the lender to the holding company and an ochre "Repayments" arrow back, agreeing with the Mermaid map's lender-to-holding financing edge; ownership arrows navy, management dashed. Alt text and caption say who lends, who borrows and who owes the repayments.
- AIB-005 (medium) — comic panel 6 → caption rewritten in short sentences: investors paid €8m for new shares at closing; €0.4m transaction costs left €7.6m; the approved plan allows four new engineering hires, so Ines, the CEO, could authorize three; Alex's original request for five was one more than the plan allows and would have needed the investor director's consent; a purchase of existing shares would have paid the seller; a corporate owner's budget needs its own approval. JSON caption, prompt "Convey:" text and visible caption kept identical (both comic generators' dry runs pass). The panel image was not regenerated: the scene is unchanged and the journal's comic rule forbids amounts on props, so the arithmetic belongs in the caption.

# Revision log — in-depth review of Same Company, Same Performance, Three Different Returns, 17 September 2026

Response to round 1 of the in-depth review (`.in-depth-reviews/20260917-161234.MGesGr/round-01.review.md`, findings TDR-001 to TDR-007 plus the terminology audit). The spec was revised before the article; permalink and title unchanged.

## 02-three-different-returns — Same Company, Same Performance, Three Different Returns (`three-different-returns`)

**Files changed:** spec.md, index.md, summary.md, comics.md, the three panel images `comic-02-scene.jpeg`, `comic-03-scene.jpeg` and `comic-04-scene.jpeg` (regenerated with `_research/generate_comics.py`, shared cast reference), the generator's own records `_research/comic-generation-prompts-20260913.json` and `_research/comic-generation-progress-20260913.json`, eight superseded panel variants archived by the generator under `_research/discarded-comic-variants/`, `docs/private-techuity/three-different-returns.html`, `.spec.html` and the three copied panel images. Panels 1, 5 and 6 and the three article/summary figures are unchanged (caption-only edits carry no numbers the artwork shows).

- TDR-001 (medium) — vocabulary barrier in the opening → **Implemented.** WHY INVESTORS CARE now opens with "An investment **fund** pools its investors' money; the fund's **manager** chooses what to buy and reports the results to those investors", then purchase price, borrowing and sale in three sentences (58 words, was one 56-word sentence). KEY POINTS use "changes in the investor's ownership percentage" and "the money the fund receives at the sale"; "at exit" removed. The preview paragraph defines buyout (a purchase of a controlling share), introduces entry/exit beside purchase/sale, and states "Amounts are in millions of euros, written €m" before the first €m table (the later duplicate sentence removed).
- TDR-002 (medium) — EBITDA explained by expansion only; not distinguished from cash → **Implemented** in all three formats. Article: interest and income taxes named; depreciation (equipment, vehicles) and amortization (acquired software, customer contracts) explained as charges spreading an asset's cost over the years it is used; "It is an earnings subtotal, not money in the bank. A company with €15 million of EBITDA still has to pay its interest and taxes, replace equipment and fund growth before any cash is left to repay debt. The €20 million repayment … is therefore a separate assumption about the cash left after that spending"; the preview paragraph carries the short form ("It is not cash in the bank" and the separate-assumption sentence); the base-case table's "Operating earnings" label replaced by "The earnings measure defined above". Summary and comic introduction carry the plain-language definition plus the not-cash and separate-assumption statements.
- TDR-003 (medium) — summary not self-contained → **Implemented.** The three outcomes are a five-column table (sale price as a multiple, business value, fund receives after €40m of debt, times its €40m, per year). Business value/enterprise value, equity value ("the owners' share") and net debt are explained at the equation; MOIC and IRR are both expanded, IRR as "the equivalent annual rate given when each payment happened"; the "third row" sentence replaced with "When both earnings and the multiple rise, part of the gain comes from their combined effect"; dilution shown as 200 of 1,000 (20%) becoming 200 of 1,250 (16%) after the company sells 250 new shares; fund, buyout, leverage and multiple (10× = €10 of price per €1 of annual EBITDA) defined.
- TDR-004 (medium) — comic assumes fund, EBITDA, multiple, equity, share issue → **Implemented.** The introduction (now three paragraphs) explains the fund, the buyout, EBITDA (as an earnings measure, not cash), the multiple, lenders repaid first, the repayment as a separate cash assumption, and that the "about x% a year" figures are compounded annual rates for one payment in and one sale receipt five years later with no intervening investor payments and no fees or sale taxes. Panel 1's caption adds "because the lenders are repaid first; the fund's share of what remains is its equity". Panel 5's caption now reads "The company then sells 250 new shares to another investor while the first investor keeps its 200 … With equal rights to the sale proceeds". Dialogue unchanged, so panels 1 and 5 keep their images.
- TDR-005 (medium) — unexplained later terms → **Implemented.** Bridge defined at first use ("a step-by-step breakdown that walks from one amount to another … assigning each step to a cause"); "claims" replaced by "who has a right to be paid from the business, when, and with how much risk" and "other rights to payment that a buyer would deduct"; refinancing ("replacing an existing loan with a new one"), maturity date ("the deadline by which a loan must be repaid") and debt-funded dividend ("borrowing in order to pay the owners now") glossed inline; pre-/post-money given as equity value before/after the new investment; "interim distributions" replaced by "payments to shareholders before the sale"; "cash bridge" and "acquisition structure" replaced by plain descriptions; "governance and operating interventions" replaced by "oversight of the company's decisions and changes to its operations".
- TDR-006 (low) — Alex and Priya unintroduced → **Implemented:** "Alex, Larkspur's technology leader, and Priya, its product leader"; "commercial chain" replaced by "the chain from action to benefit" and "integrated" by "connected between the two products".
- TDR-007 (low) — panels 2–4 show three different operating charts → **Implemented, adapted.** Prompts for the three panels now describe one chart: a framed chart on an easel with a single straight EARNINGS line rising left to right and exactly five plain dark DEBT bars shrinking left to right, labels written once, no numbers; only the price tag (10×/12×/7×) and the people's poses differ. Three generation passes (panel 3 accepted on pass two; panels 2 and 4 on pass three after a four-bar chart and an off-model bald Sam were rejected); the final read-back shows the same chart structure in all three panels with each bubble's words correct. Alt text updated to "a chart with a rising earnings line and shrinking debt bars". The review's request for €10m→€15m and €60m→€40m endpoint labels on the artwork was not followed: the journal's generator forbids amounts on props (so that no panel invents numbers) and the captions carry those figures; recorded as adapted.
- Terminology audit → addressed where the audience is a company leader without finance background: buyout, entry/exit, fund/manager, holding period, share, share issue, minority investment, leverage placement of the debt, MOIC in plain words ("how many times the investor got its money back"), fee/profit share/pay-out rules in the fund-economics pointer, "SSRN" expanded, venture investors and buyouts described in the reading-list annotations. Ordinary technology terms (onboarding, implementation, platform work, deployment report) are left as they are for the stated audience of product and engineering leaders.

**Adapted:** TDR-007 (no numeric endpoint labels on artwork, see above). The summary was retrimmed to 498 prose words (figure line and caption excluded; the five-row table adds 71 words) after the added definitions, mainly by shortening the base-case sentences and using €m throughout. timetoread 14 → 16 min (3,307 → 3,758 body words at the rate that gave 14).
**Declined:** the audit's optional suggestion to relabel "UNSOLD VALUE" as an estimate on the summary overview image was not taken up; it is not a finding, the caption already says "distinguish cash from estimated value", and regenerating the overview would change an image no finding fault with.
**Unresolved / needs separate action:** none for this article. `python3 _wiring/build.py` also regenerated `docs/private-techuity/announcement-is-not-a-budget.html` from that chapter's uncommitted working-tree source; that generated page was restored to HEAD so this change set stays scoped to this article, and it will be rebuilt whenever that chapter's edit is finished. `_research/artwork-review-20260913.json` is a dated acceptance record and was left as is.
**Verification:** arithmetic recomputed (7×/10×/12× → EV €105m/€150m/€180m, equity €65m/€110m/€140m, MOIC 1.625×/2.75×/3.5×, IRR 10.197%/22.424%/28.474%; gain €70m = 175% of €40m, split €50m + €20m; interaction €50m + €20m + €10m = €80m; 2× in three/seven years 25.99%/10.41%; downside €80m − €60m = €20m, business −20%, fund −50%; dilution 200/1,000 = 20%, 200/1,250 = 16%, 16% × €20m = €3.2m = 1.6× on €2m; pre-/post-money €8m/€10m). Five cross-link targets exist and the built page has no unresolved `[[…]]`. Comic generator dry run passes (caption ⊂ prompt, visible caption identical, six panels, JSON valid); every panel's recorded sha256 matches its file; no `needs_regeneration` flag. `python3 _wiring/build.py` printed 21 `[built]` lines; the rendered payload contains the article, TL;DR and Comic texts and the View spec link, and the spec page shows the accepted chip. Screenshots with `npx playwright screenshot` (Playwright 1.50, bundled Chromium) at 1280×900 and 390×844 for the article, `#summary` and `#comics` tabs: title, byline (16 min read, View spec), hero, tabs, the summary table (with the "Scroll sideways" cue at phone width) and all six comic panels render; no overflow or missing image.

## 02-three-different-returns — Same Company, Same Performance, Three Different Returns (`three-different-returns`)

No image, comic panel, prompt record or config change was needed; the review inspected all 11 images and raised no media finding.

- TDR-003 (medium) — summary presents "EBITDA × multiple" as the definition of enterprise value and omits the return assumptions → **Implemented.** Summary now reads "**Business value**, or enterprise value, is the value of the operating business; this example prices it at annual EBITDA × the assumed multiple." The paragraph before the three-outcome table ends with "The table assumes no cash offsetting the debt, no further investor money paid in or out before the sale, unchanged ownership, and no fees or sale taxes:". The €65m/€110m/€140m proceeds, 1.6×/2.75×/3.5× and about 10%/22%/28% per year are unchanged and were recomputed (EV €105m/€150m/€180m − €40m; IRR 10.20%/22.42%/28.47%). The terminology audit's optional "separate downside" label was also taken: "In a separate downside case, business value down a fifth to €80m with €60m still owed would halve the fund's money: leverage **magnifies losses too**."
- TDR-008 (low) — 177-word preview paragraph, 153-word EBITDA paragraph and 137-word summary setup each do several jobs → **Implemented.** Article preview split into three paragraphs: purchase funding with buyout/entry/exit (53 words); five-year performance, the short EBITDA reading and the separate cash assumption (78 words); what is fixed and the one variable that changes, plus the €m unit (46 words). The full EBITDA explanation is split between the excluded charges (89 words, ending at "financing, tax and asset history") and a separate paragraph on why EBITDA is not available cash and why the €20m repayment is a separate assumption (64 words). Summary setup split the same way: buyout and leverage (42 words); EBITDA, not cash, five-year result and repayment assumption (66 words); the one variable plus the assumptions sentence (54 words). Wording otherwise preserved; no sentence was dropped.

**Adapted:** none.
**Declined:** none.
**Unresolved / needs separate action:** the summary prose is now 535 words (table, figure line and caption excluded), above the spec's 300–500-word guidance by about 35 words after the requested assumptions sentence and enterprise-value definition; five small trims were made ("borrowing less cash counted in the deal", "So …", "reports both cash returned and estimates of unsold holdings;" etc.) without removing any qualification. Trimming further would mean cutting a definition the round-1 and round-2 reviews asked for, so the overage is recorded rather than forced.
**Verification:** arithmetic recomputed for the three-multiple table, the €70m/175% gain, the €50m + €20m + €10m interaction and the €80m − €60m downside; `python3 _wiring/build.py` printed 21 `[built]` lines and, compared against a pre-build snapshot of `docs/`, changed only this post's page and spec page; the built payload carries index, summary and comics with zero unresolved `[[…]]` and all four in-page `.html` links resolving; Playwright (Chromium) screenshots at 390×844 and 1280×900 of the Article and TL;DR tabs show the new paragraph breaks, the assumptions sentence directly above the summary table, and the phone-width sideways-scroll cue on both tables. The untouched working-tree edits to the announcement chapter and its generated page were left as found.

Summary prose trimmed from 535 to 500 words (source text, table, figure line and caption excluded, each `[[…]]` counted as one word): dropped the "Calling 7× a failure, or 12× proof of exceptional engineering, is the same mistake in opposite directions" sentence as framing repeated by the bridge paragraph and the closing claim (replaced by "Proceeds more than double, although nothing about the company changed."), dropped "Two extensions.", and shortened "expressed as a multiple", "no further investor money paid in or out", "when each payment happened" → "the payment dates", "this example prices it at" → "priced here at". Every figure, definition and assumption is still present; "profit share" now reads "the manager's profit share" (audit note).
- TDR-009 (low) — "platform work", "onboarding" and "implementation effort" unexplained → **Implemented.** "**Platform work** means improving the shared software foundations on which the company's products are built, such as the systems that store data or handle logins; customers rarely see it directly." added directly after the €30m claim; "Better onboarding might reduce implementation effort and allow additional sales. Improved reliability might reduce customer losses." replaced by "Making the product easier for new customers to set up might reduce installation and training work and allow additional sales. Fewer outages might reduce customer losses." The observed-improvement versus proven-contribution distinction ("Neither automatically creates the value assumed in a spreadsheet …") is unchanged.

## 26-valuation-is-an-estimate — A Valuation Is an Estimate, Not a Fact (`valuation-is-an-estimate`)

**Files changed:** spec.md (two success criteria, `revised:` and changelog), index.md, summary.md, comics.md, three images regenerated with the journal's generators (`summary-at-a-glance.jpeg` via `_research/generate_summary_visuals.py`; `comic-03-scene.jpeg` and `comic-06-scene.jpeg` via `_research/generate_comics.py` with the shared cast reference), the generators' own records (`_research/summary-visual-prompts-20260913.json`, `_research/comic-generation-prompts-20260913.json`, `_research/comic-generation-progress-20260913.json`, `_research/comic-visual-corrections-20260913.json`), the three superseded images archived by the generators under `_research/discarded-summary-variants/` and `_research/discarded-comic-variants/`, this log, and the rebuilt `docs/private-techuity/valuation-is-an-estimate.html`, `.spec.html` and the three copied images. Panels 1, 2, 4 and 5, both article figures, the logo and the icon are unchanged (caption-only edits on panels 2 and 4 carry nothing the artwork shows; dialogue unchanged).

- VAL-001 (medium) — reverse bridge says "add back the borrowing" → **Fixed.** "If €60 million is instead the equity value, adding back the same €20 million of net debt gives €80 million of enterprise value, before those other adjustments." Both directions recomputed (60 − 20 = 40; 60 + 20 = 80). The "transaction adjustments" caveat now carries one example (an agreed correction for the working cash left in the business at completion).
- VAL-002 (medium) — WHY INVESTORS CARE assumes every investor reports to funders → **Fixed** with the review's scope: "An investor managing money for others may need to report an estimated value of the company and explain the assumptions behind it. - VAL-003 (medium) — terms before explanation → **Fixed.** Margin defined in WHY YOU SHOULD CARE (profit as a percentage of sales); "claims" in KEY POINTS replaced by "other rights to be paid from the business before the shareholders" and at the EV definition by "who has a right to be paid from it: lenders, shareholders or others"; SEC expanded and its role stated, non-GAAP/GAAP explained; "liabilities" at the method check replaced by "debts and other obligations" (the defined use at the asset-based section stays); onboarding and implementation defined in the closing example; "cohort" replaced by "a first group of customers to prove that it works" and "two quarters (six months)"; "engineer-weeks" by "weeks of engineers' time"; reading annotations now explain balance sheet, IFRS, fair value, unicorn, share class and preferred shares. Also from the audit: "recognizes" → "records"; interest as "the charge for borrowing money"; IPEV expanded at its first citation and "private investments" glossed; "expensed"/"qualifying" development reworded; "refinanced (replaced with a new loan)"; "subscribes" → "pays the company €2m for newly issued shares"; "other securities" → "any other instruments that could later convert into shares"; "earnings base" → "earnings figure"; "capital" → "funding"; "parent" and "migration/two platforms" put in plain words; "self-service data import" → "a data import that customers can run themselves".
- VAL-004 (medium) — summary and comic not self-contained → **Fixed.** Summary: €16 million of operating expenses "(excluding depreciation and amortization)"; assets exemplified (equipment or software) and asset-based valuation as "adds up what the business owns (its assets) and subtracts what it owes"; pre-money/post-money defined as the value of the shares just before/immediately after new funding; "pays €2m for newly issued shares"; margin glossed. Comic: introduction defines valuation and margin; panel 2 caption expands EBITDA as an earnings measure, names interest as the cost of borrowing, exemplifies assets and distinguishes an "adjusted" EBITDA (further costs the company chooses; ask what each is and whether it recurs); panel 3 caption exemplifies assets and states the subtraction of what is owed; panel 4 names revenue (sales) and EBITDA as example measures; panel 6 caption defines pre-/post-money, says the investor pays for newly issued shares and that the company's cash increases by €2m rather than receiving €10m.

# Revision log — in-depth review of Match the Funding to the Work, 17 September 2026

Response to round 1 of the in-depth review (`.in-depth-reviews/20260917-172341.VpdY3Z/round-01.review.md`, findings RWN-001 to RWN-009 plus the terminology audit). The spec (success criteria, `revised:` and changelog) was updated before the article; permalink, id and title unchanged; the loan schedule, the €610,000 need and every previously published number are unchanged.

## 03-raise-what-you-need — Match the Funding to the Work (`raise-what-you-need`)

**Files changed:** spec.md, index.md, summary.md, comics.md, the regenerated panel image `comic-06-scene.jpeg` (via `_research/generate_comics.py`, shared cast reference; the superseded panel archived by the generator under `_research/discarded-comic-variants/03-raise-what-you-need-comic-06-scene-54dbcb3f2a17.jpeg`) and the regenerated article figure `one-product-different-funding-plans.jpeg` (via the article-illustrator script from a staged placeholder, `--overwrite --replace`), the generator records `_research/comic-generation-prompts-20260913.json` and `comic-generation-progress-20260913.json` (updated by the generator, with the panel’s previous prompt kept), the figure record `_research/article-illustration-prompts-20260913.json` (new prompt, alt, caption and sha256 for this figure with the previous prompt and hash kept), this log, and the rebuilt `docs/private-techuity/raise-what-you-need.html`, `raise-what-you-need.spec.html` and the two copied images. Panels 1–5, Figure 2, the summary overview, logo and icon are unchanged as files.

- RWN-001 (high) — annual-flow shortfall confused with inability to pay; year-one €290,000 unexplained; reserve arithmetic contradicted → **Implemented.** New stated assumptions: Larkspur holds €250,000, the board’s minimum balance is €200,000, so €50,000 is unrestricted. New “Cash balance, base case and downside” table (cash above the minimum, years 1–4): opening €50,000; loan €750,000; operating cash €350,000 a year; work paid for €420,000 in year one (build, transition, twelve months of the contract specialist) and €30,000 in year two (first maintenance year, the financial year after the step ships); payments €60,000 / €310,000 / €290,000 / €270,000; closing €670,000 / €680,000 / €740,000 / €820,000; still held for the work €330,000 / €300,000 (unspent allowance and runway, maintenance still to pay, the €140,000 reserve; released after the eighteen funded months); downside closing €670,000 / €630,000 / €640,000 / €670,000. The year-one margin is retained cash and is what covers the downside. The €10,000 is now described as a year-two operating-cash deficit covered from retained cash, not a missed payment; on the forecast no payment is missed in either case, and the text says what would make one missed. Consuming the cushion is distinguished from underfunding the work: using €10,000 of the reserve for a payment leaves the €610,000 funded and the cushion at €130,000 (the former “short by the same amount” sentence removed). Maintenance after the funded period stated as an ordinary operating cost inside the €350,000 forecast from year three. The front-loading paragraph now reconciles with the same figures (€345,000 of spending against about €175,000 of operating cash in the first two quarters and €50,000 unrestricted: about €120,000 below the required balance by month six). Sam’s strict test (each year’s payment from that year’s operating cash alone) is kept and labelled as the conservative rule; the “Revisit” section now says the year-two margin has retained year-one cash and the reserve behind it.
- RWN-002 (medium) — amber fallback needs four engineer-weeks the money-only reserve cannot supply → **Implemented.** New paragraph “Who does the work, and when”: the twelve engineer-weeks are three engineers for four weeks (step usable from about day 35; the implementation team sets up the eight cohort customers between day 35 and day 90); the €40,000 comes from the reserve and buys a data-validation service plus the contract specialist’s time correcting customer records; the four engineer-weeks are two of the same three engineers for two weeks from day 100, which moves the reporting improvements they were due to start back by two weeks; the step is ready by about month four, the second cohort of eight is set up in months four to six and measured at month six; the internal and contract specialists stay on the transition and the cohorts are set up by the implementation team, so no one is booked twice. The need table and the scarce-capacity paragraph now say engineer-weeks are effort, not elapsed time. The amber rule no longer says the engineer-weeks come “from the €140,000 reserve”.
- RWN-003 (medium) — assumed finance vocabulary → **Implemented** in all three formats. Summary and comic: see RWN-005 and RWN-006.

## 04-obligations-before-budget — Confirm the Cash Before You Commit (`obligations-before-budget`)

In-depth review, 17 September 2026. Round 1 (`round-01.review.md`, findings OBB-001 to OBB-009 plus the terminology audit).

**Files changed:** spec.md (six success criteria added; changelog line; `revised:` 2026-09-17), index.md, summary.md, comics.md, the regenerated panel images `comic-02-scene.jpeg` and `comic-06-scene.jpeg` (via `_research/generate_comics.py` with the shared cast reference; superseded variants archived by the generator under `_research/discarded-comic-variants/04-obligations-before-budget-*`), the generator records `_research/comic-generation-prompts-20260913.json` and `comic-generation-progress-20260913.json` (this post’s panel 2 and 6 entries only), the built pages `docs/private-techuity/obligations-before-budget.html`, `obligations-before-budget.spec.html`, `index.html` (this post’s excerpt only) and the two docs image copies, and the manuscript export `manuscripts/owned/obligations-before-budget.md` with `export-manifest.json`.

- OBB-001 (medium) — finance vocabulary met before usable explanations → **Fixed.** Excerpt, the three highlights and KEY POINTS rewritten in ordinary language (“positive earnings are not money you can spend”, “payments the company must make / the ones it chooses”, “payouts to owners”). Opening explains operating earnings, migration and reconciliation at first use; a new three-paragraph terms passage defines EBITDA with interest, depreciation and amortization explained locally (“spread the cost of an asset across the years it is used”), capex/capitalization and working capital before the bridge. Buyout, distributions, shareholders’ agreement, corporate parent, waiver, amendment, refinancing (in the authority table), revenue, unrestricted cash, funding round, one-off costs, leverage ratio and net debt (with a €60m − €5m over €10m = 5.5× example), value-creation plan, IAS 38 (expanded, intangible asset explained), expensed, adjusted EBITDA, SEC (expanded, role stated), non-GAAP (expanded), restructuring charge and recurring acquisition costs (plain descriptions), private equity, sponsor, fund commitments (“how much money the sponsor’s fund can still draw from its own investors”), operating cash flow and fiscal 2016’s end date (28 January 2017) explained; “preferred instrument” replaced by “a class of shares with priority payment rights”; “statutory” by “required by law”; the leverage covenant defined in the assumptions paragraph before the options table uses it. Summary carries its own definitions (EBITDA with interest and D&A, working capital, onboarding, runway, covenants, capitalization, roles). Comic introduction defines EBITDA, working capital and net burn; panel 1 explains migration; panel 4 uses plain wording; panel 5 defines net burn and states the €100,000 as monthly.
- OBB-002 (medium) — worked decision imports unstated context → **Fixed.** New “The project” paragraph (manual onboarding, 80 staff hours per customer, 50 target, the reusable setup step, Ines as chief executive, Sam finance, Priya product, Alex engineering); “The pilot’s cost, staffing and dates” paragraph (engineer-week defined as effort not elapsed time; three engineers for four weeks; step usable from about day 35; eight cohort customers set up between day 35 and day 90; Priya measures at day 90; cohort defined; board and delegation explained). The former 200-word decision paragraph split into “The choice”, a four-bullet “The decision record”, a six-step “The sequence” and “What would change the decision”. Scenario disclaimers consolidated into one sentence in the bridge paragraph.
- OBB-003 (medium) — fallback affordability relied on the original forecast → **Fixed.** “The fallback’s cash check” states its assumptions (residual ≈ €125,000 a quarter; remaining €90,000 build paid in two €45,000 instalments at the ends of Q2 and Q3; maintenance €2,500 a month from entry into service; one-quarter specialist €37,500) and gives two dated checks: day 100 with collections on plan (€300,000 + €125,000 − €90,000 = €335,000 above the reserve against at most €157,500 still to pay) and mid-year with weak collections (original €0.8m set aside; €135,000 paid; €97,500 still to pay; €300,000 + €250,000 − shortfall − €135,000: €265,000 at a €150,000 shortfall, the fallback fits; €15,000 at a €400,000 shortfall, it does not, so spending reduction, board reserve draw or new funding, plus a covenant recheck). The fallback is stated as conditional on the revised forecast covering each payment date.
- OBB-004 (medium) — inconsistent second-stage chronology → **Fixed.** One baseline: day 90 is evidence, day 100 the board decides what comes next and releases no second-stage money, the month-six gate (≤ 50 hours with waiting time down) exists only if the board schedules it, the next annual budget round is the default forum, and any second stage needs its own priced, funded approval. Table cell, sequence list and “What would change the decision” agree; a strong result is now “the case for asking the board at day 100 to schedule the month-six review rather than wait for the budget round”. Consistent with the canonical ledger’s day-100 rule (expansion decision moved to the next quarterly review).
- OBB-005 (medium) — summary compression changed the decision → **Fixed.** Options paragraph rewritten around the selected €210,000 pilot fitting the €0.8m capacity (about €0.6m left), approval distinguished from the day-90 measurement and the day-100 decision (“releasing no second-stage money”), a strong result “may earn a month-six gate; any second stage needs its own priced approval”, the fallback conditional on Sam’s revised forecast with half the build paid and half committed (not “already spent”). Summary prose 497 words (image line and caption excluded, `[[…]]` tokens stripped). The historical sponsor-support sentence was dropped from the summary rather than kept with a source; it remains, sourced, in the article.
- OBB-006 (medium) — panel 2’s “UNPAID INVOICES” tray read as another bill → **Fixed.** Panel 2 regenerated: Sam fills trays labelled INTEREST, TAX and ASSETS while an empty tray labelled CUSTOMERS HAVE NOT PAID waits for coins still between a grey customer holding an INVOICE and the desk; dialogue “Some of this cash has not arrived yet.”; caption states that the earnings figure counts sales whose invoices customers have not yet paid; alt text updated. Image inspected.
- OBB-007 (medium) — panel 6 did not show the decision → **Fixed, adapted.** Panel 6 regenerated (three attempts: the first added Morgan, the second turned the labels toward Ines; the third accepted): Ines signs a PILOT sheet with KEEP GOING, PILOT and FULL PROGRAM sheets facing the reader, Alex and Priya present, one calendar marked BOARD and a later GATE; dialogue “The pilot fits. The rest needs evidence.” Amounts stay in the caption, as the generator forbids figures on props: €75,000 / €210,000 (€180,000 + €30,000) / €1 million against about €800,000, Ines’s approval within her delegated limit, about €600,000 left, the day-100 board review releasing no further money, and the month-six gate as the undecided part; “settle the rest” removed. Alt text and storyboard prompt synchronized. Image inspected.
- OBB-008 (low) — accounting table without units → **Fixed.** Header now “Measure (illustrative amounts, € millions) | Expensed | Capitalized”.
- OBB-009 (low) — crisis study’s geography omitted → **Fixed.** “almost 500 UK companies backed by private equity before the 2008 crisis with similar UK companies without such backing, matched on industry, size, profitability and borrowing … stronger where the sponsors had more uninvested money … evidence of a conditional support mechanism in one country and one crisis, not a promise of rescue” (checked against the authors’ Harvard Law School Forum account).
- Terminology audit → addressed as listed under OBB-001. Left as they were: “TL;DR” tab label (journal-wide), CEO/CFO/CTO in the comic’s internal cast metadata, source identifiers (S06, S07, S10, S35; link text is descriptive), and the decorative ledger/statement labels in existing panel artwork.

Reconciliation: first-quarter engagement → operating plan (cash and days, shared between the setup queue and the build); build → €180,000 with no specialist days; extension → option 1 (€75,000, two quarters) or the fallback (€37,500, one quarter). - OBB-014 (medium) — opening shorthand subtracted the whole amount customers owe → **Fixed.** Excerpt, the investors highlight and the first key point now say “any increase in what customers still owe” / “any growth in unpaid customer invoices”; the working-capital passage states that the bridge adjusts for the change over the year, not the whole balance, with the requested contrast (€2 million owed at the start and €3 million at the end is a €1 million adjustment, not €3 million), says cash comes back only when customers pay, and distinguishes a write-off (reduces the amount owed, brings in nothing); “a decrease releases it” removed. Summary opening now reads “any increase in unpaid customer invoices”. Comic introduction says “the sales customers have not yet paid for”; panel 2’s caption already described unpaid invoices as money owed to the company and is unchanged.
- OBB-010 (medium) — specialist availability after 31 March unestablished → **Fixed.** New labelled paragraph “The specialist after March”: Sam adds a no-charge option to the first-quarter engagement (extend once, three months, same €37,500, by notice on or before day 100; no reservation fee or interim cost; the specialist is unpaid between 31 March and the notice; the option lapses unused); exercised at day 100 the specialist returns mid-April to mid-July, invoiced at €12,500 a month; option 1 chosen at approval starts on 1 April with no gap; the mid-year fallback has no option, so on 30 June Sam asks the firm for the same specialist or a briefed replacement at the same rate, and if neither is available the implementation team carries the peak with the step as it stands and the expansion question goes to the budget round. Each fallback is now conditional on both an available specialist and cash (at-a-glance table, decision record “Fallback arranged” bullet, sequence step 4, “What would change the decision”, check one’s opening, check two’s payment list and closing condition; summary fallback sentence). Check two also states that it assumes the day-100 option was not exercised. No amounts or payment dates changed, so the €150,000 / €152,500 and the 30 June–30 September schedule stand.
- OBB-012 (medium) — lender consent inferred from a periodic test → **Fixed.** The assumptions paragraph adds a fictional loan term: no new borrowing above a small permitted amount without the existing lender’s consent, “whatever the ratio shows on the test date”; the leverage covenant’s cap is stated net of countable cash. Option 3’s approver cell now names the existing lender (consent required by that term) and, separately, a lender willing to make the new loan; the rejection sentence says the same. The covenant section distinguishes covenants tested periodically from those restricting actions at any time and refers back to option 3. Ines’s delegated limit, the board and the shareholders remain mapped as before.
- OBB-013 (medium) — dense passages → **Fixed.** The opening’s second recap of the chapter’s plan removed (one sentence now hands off from [[valuation-is-an-estimate]]). The calendar paragraph split into “The calendar” (dates), “The maintenance charge” (the €27,500 / €2,500 split and the €30,000 reserved at approval) and “Who approves” (delegation; €210,000 within it; €1 million above it; board decides at day 100). The day-100 check is a nine-row table (€300,000 + €125,000 − €90,000 − €5,000 = €330,000; €90,000 + €37,500 + €22,500 = €150,000) followed by the €152,500 note, a separate three-qualification paragraph (pilot payments only; operating obligations met from the operating plan; quarter-end residual as a timing convention) and a one-sentence conclusion. The sponsor-evidence paragraph split into three (sponsor definition; the Bernstein–Lerner–Mezzanotti finding with its scope; planning implication). Comic panel 6’s caption split into the immediate choice (99 words) and the later review (39 words), with the prompt’s Convey text updated to match; artwork unchanged and re-inspected (Ines signing PILOT beside KEEP GOING and FULL PROGRAM, Alex and Priya, BOARD and GATE calendar).

- DWD-001 (high) — pilot calendar conflicted with the cash chapter → **Fixed, adapted.** Took the review’s second option: the pilot (approved by Ines on 1 January, usable early February, eight customers measured by 1 April) is now distinguished from *completing the first stage* (the less-common customer-type templates and every new customer on the step), which is the delivery Alex dates: October with two June starts, January of the following year with the current team. The completion’s scope, funding (inside the €210,000: the €180,000 build includes the contractor template time, paid on the cash plan’s dates; maintenance €2,500 a month from February) and capacity (the implementation team on payroll plus the twelve engineer-weeks) are stated. The “first three pilot implementations” evidence is replaced by Priya’s 1 April eight-customer report plus Sam’s first-quarter collections, so one evidence set serves the April review and the May meeting. The board’s April review (about day 100) now dates the reporting example, and the update says the hiring decision “stays with the May meeting, where the January record placed it” instead of “the next quarterly review”. A nine-step dated sequence (day counts in brackets, matching the cash chapter’s day 0 / 35 / 90 / 100) closes the section. “January of the following year” is used instead of a numeric year because the shared scenario names no calendar year. Summary and comic carry the same calendar.
- DWD-002 (medium) — final record dropped the June-start condition and left the specialist unscheduled → **Fixed.** “Conditions for October: board approval at the May meeting, and two confirmed June start dates.” New “What the hires change” (they take over part of the setup queue so the implementation team can move customers onto the step) and “The specialist” paragraphs: days come out of the first-quarter engagement ending 31 March; one-quarter extension at €37,500 exercisable by 10 April within Ines’s delegation (the cash chapter’s option); third-quarter availability asked on 30 June; specialist-dependent templates specified before mid-July on either path. Four branches written out: no decision (escalate or confirm January, team told in writing), approval granted but no June starts (date reverts to January until both start dates are confirmed), approval refused (January; hiring waits for the budget round), specialist unavailable after mid-July (January stands; October depended on the hires). “By May” replaced by “at its May meeting” / “the May meeting passes” throughout.
- DWD-003 (medium) — unexplained financial terms → **Fixed.** Reserve, envelope, approved plan and headcount plan explained where the delegation rule is stated; forecast surplus and opening cash in plain words; collections, cash profiles, bridge, funding round, term sheet, carried interest, material and benchmark each explained at first meaningful use; fund and investment committee, lender (with a loan-consent example), private equity, value creation and KKR (“a large investment firm”) explained in the decision-makers paragraph.

- DWD-002 (medium) — specialist capacity was optional, not secured → **Fixed.** The baseline is now executable inside the €210,000: the specialist’s first-quarter engagement (ending 31 March) includes the days spent writing the less-common configurations down for the build, and Alex accepts that written record, or names its gaps, by 31 March; the January plan relies on the record, not on the specialist staying. The extension is a contingency stated as additional spending: one quarter at €37,500 by notice on or before day 100 (11 April); pilot plus extension €247,500 before any hiring or the €40,000 data-quality step; within Ines’s delegation; three monthly invoices of €12,500 from mid-April with the last due in July; exercised only if Sam’s day-100 rerun shows cash above the reserve on each date (the cash chapter’s condition).
