# Revision log — in-depth review of Management Equity, Fund Carry and Employee Jobs Are Different Bets, 18 September 2026

## 06-different-bets — Management Equity, Fund Carry and Employee Jobs Are Different Bets (`different-bets`)

In-depth review, run `20260918-080316.HUtbL5` round 1 (`round-01.review.md`, findings DB-001 to DB-010 plus the terminology audit), applied 18 September 2026. Spec updated first (`spec.md`: one closure timeline across formats with the mechanism of the renewal-window risk, decision gates and pause costs, the three-case commission rule, single-question table cells and decision items, plan-specific severance and reference commitments, the comic’s new panel order; changelog line; `revised:` 2026-09-18). No scenario amount changed: €600,000, €350,000, €120,000, €80,000, €550,000, 70%, €6 million, €300,000, about €4 million and about €800,000 are as before.

**Files changed:** spec.md, index.md (`timetoread` 14 → 25 min: 4,926 prose words before the questions, tables and image lines excluded, at 200 wpm), summary.md (495 words by the collection’s counter), comics.md (rewritten intro, six captions and alts, new order), comic artwork (see DB-004/DB-005), `_research/comic-generation-prompts-20260913.json` (all six `06-different-bets/*` entries: prompt, dialogue, alt, caption, sha256 and a `revision_note`), `_research/comic-generation-progress-20260913.json` (overwritten by the generator with this run’s result, as it always is), three archived images under `_research/discarded-comic-variants/`, the rebuilt `docs/private-techuity/` pages and panel copies, and `manuscripts/owned/different-bets.md` with the export manifest.

- DB-001 (high) — the chronology did not explain why staging protects the renewals, and “lose their jobs now” conflicted with the dated plan → **Fixed.** New subsection “Why the Nine-Month Closure Endangers the Renewals” states that nobody’s employment ends before month nine and names the three risks that begin at the announcement: unprotected specialists free to resign, an add-on handover of about six months that must start by month three to close at month nine, and customers asked to renew before anyone can name who will know their setup. A month-by-month table sets the rejected and chosen plans side by side (announcement, renewals in months 1–6, gates, notices, departures, premises, month from which each saving runs). The staged plan keeps configuration work with the three specialists and five colleagues until after the last renewal, moves only routine questions in months four to six (said plainly, with the waiting-time trigger watching it), and schedules a per-customer walkthrough before each renewal. Being told at the announcement is distinguished from formal notice (two months in this scenario, as is giving up a floor). The four-bets table, summary and comic now say the eleven “are told now that their jobs will end”, month nine under the proposal, month six (six people) and month twelve (five) under the chosen plan. Departure dates did not change, so the savings arithmetic is unchanged.
- DB-002 (medium) — vocabulary before explanation → **Fixed.** Opening callouts and key points rewritten in ordinary words; a short “A few words first” paragraph introduces shares, shareholders, equity, management equity, investor, fund, fund manager and exit; fees and carry are explained in the next paragraph. Rollover, options, exercising, share awards, vesting, share classes, preferences, ordinary shares, dilution, funding round and leaver provisions are all defined before the first table. Threshold, waterfall, refinancing, acquisition, venture-funded, buyout, corporate parent, integration milestones, valuation, general partner, fees, governance, investment partner, chief technology officer (CTO), revenue, earnings, adjusted EBITDA (in plain words, with “adjusted” explained), onboarding, guardrail, contribution, market multiples, consolidation, payroll, premises, severance, configurations, preferred return, retention payments, operating cash, engineer-week, annual run rate, lenders and margin are explained at first use. A reminder paragraph before the four-bets table explains the earnings multiple, enterprise value and equity value (enterprise value minus debt plus cash). “Bridge”, “hurdle”, “nominal”, “fee economics”, “financial sponsor”, “hypothesis”, “term sheets”, “time horizon” and “Gross value after …” were replaced with plain wording. NBER and PE are spelled out in To Probe Further.
- DB-003 (medium) — summary relied on term lists → **Fixed.** Fund, exit, management equity and carry are explained in the summary’s own words; the list “vesting, preferences, dilution” became “who is paid first in a sale, when the rights are earned, how many new shares are issued and what happens if the holder leaves”; gross revenue retention now has its denominator; contribution became “earn more in their first year than they cost to serve”; the closure is labelled fictional, the value estimate states “with debt and cash unchanged”, and the manager’s carry “comes out of that €4 million”. The option example and the diversification sentence were dropped from the summary to hold the cap (both remain in the article).
- DB-004 (medium) — comic not independently readable, ending on an unrelated calculation → **Fixed.** New order: (1) a percentage is not a payment, with shares, equity and vesting explained and the threshold example; (2) the separate option example, with option and exercising explained; (3) two payout systems; (4) a target changes behavior, without the word retention; (5) the proposed closure, the exit and the six-month renewals, with “horizon” explained; (6) the staged decision, the checks and the eleven who still carry the cost. Artwork: the former panel 6 image is now panel 2, former 3 is panel 4 and former 4 is panel 5 (files renumbered, images unchanged, each inspected again against its new caption); panel 6 is new artwork (Ines at a STAGE 1 / PAUSE / STAGE 2 planner, three employees with plain boxes, Priya watching; dialogue “Who still carries the cost?”), which replaces the former panel 5 and its unexplained labels (return on investment, quarterly gain, asset growth, profit share report, cost benefit analysis). Panel 1’s “vesting schedule” and “conditions” labels are now explained in its caption and named in its alt.
- DB-005 (medium) — panel 2 joined the two payout systems on one sheet, with “INVESTMENT SHARES SHARE” and “MGR.” → **Fixed.** New panel 3 artwork: two unconnected easel boards, COMPANY SALE (building → SHAREHOLDERS) and FUND PROFITS (money bag → FUND INVESTORS and FUND MANAGER), no connector between them, dialogue “Two sets of rules. Two payouts.” Inspected; a first variant with a dollar sign on the money bag was discarded and regenerated without a currency symbol. The old image is archived as `06-different-bets-comic-02-scene-e8335f481155.jpeg`.
- DB-006 (medium) — commission rule still paid for the damaging sale → **Fixed.** Three cases written into the plan before the year starts: positive first-year contribution pays in full at signature; a loss-making contract needs the finance leader’s prior approval with the reason recorded, pays half at signature and the other half at first renewal only if the renewed contract’s contribution is positive; an unapproved loss-making contract earns nothing. A €10,000 walk-through covers the profitable sale (€10,000), the approved loss-maker that does not renew (€5,000) and the one that renews while still losing money (€5,000), and says why the first half is an intentional exception. Loss-making contracts never count toward the executives’ growth target.
- DB-007 (medium) — dense containers → **Fixed.** The guardrail list holds one idea per bullet; the retention definition, the worked 88% / 103% example and the Fastly source note are three separate paragraphs; the four-bets table keeps offer, risk and clock, with the payout arithmetic in a numbered list below it; the recorded decision is a list (chosen, rejected, people, cost and funding, who approves, scarce capacity, gates, two triggers, who may pause and what it costs); the first-year cash comparison is two bullets and two short paragraphs. Sam, Ines and Priya are introduced by role where they first act.
- DB-008 (medium) — pause presented without a mechanism → **Fixed.** Gates sit before the two irreversible steps (formal notice, giving up a floor): end of month four and end of month ten. Renewal trigger defined (fewer than 90% of the add-on renewals that have fallen due since the announcement; 90% is last year’s rate over the same two quarters); waiting-time trigger defined as add-on support first-answer time over target two months running. The board’s approval lets Ines delay a stage by up to three months; longer, or dropping a stage, returns to the board. Each month of delay gives up €25,000 of saving, and a delayed stage two adds about €10,000 a month of retention, both from the operating budget that carries those costs today. A closing paragraph works the two test cases (renewals lost in month six; waiting time breached in months nine and ten) and says what a gate can no longer stop. The savings schedule is labelled as the no-pause case; the finance leader confirms the dated cash plan covers each payment ([[obligations-before-budget]]).
- DB-009 (medium) — “Severance and a reference are obligations” → **Fixed.** “In this plan Larkspur commits to severance and a reference for each of them. What the law or their contracts require depends on the country and the contract, and the plan’s commitments may go beyond it.” The point that neither makes up for an interrupted career is kept.
- DB-010 (low) — Metrick–Yasuda annotation → **Fixed.** Now: estimated manager revenue from the contract terms of 238 funds raised 1993–2006; about two-thirds from fixed components in that historical sample; about what managers earn, not investors’ returns. Checked against the abstract of the published paper.
- Terminology audit → handled under DB-002 to DB-004. Carry is no longer described as “normally” whole-fund: the text says many agreements compute it that way, others pay deal by deal, and ILPA recommends the whole-fund calculation as best practice. Figure 1’s “Impacts share value” label is qualified in its caption (dilution lowers the percentage; whether it lowers the money depends on the new shares’ price); Figure 2’s caption now spells out the three pairs, including “safe change”. Left as they were: the title, the S-identifiers, the “TL;DR” tab label (journal-wide), and the artwork of Figures 1 and 2, the summary overview, the logo and the icon (the review found them accurate).

**Adapted:** DB-001 (the dates were kept and the mechanism, notice rule and work split were added, rather than moving departures). DB-004 (three existing images reused under new numbers instead of regenerating all six). Figure 1 (caption qualification instead of regenerating an otherwise accurate figure).
**Declined:** none.
**Unresolved / needs separate action:** the article grew from about 3,500 to about 5,650 words including tables, most of it the timeline, gates, commission cases and first-use definitions the review asked for. The three four-column tables and the three-column timeline scroll sideways at phone width with the template’s “Scroll sideways” hint (existing template behaviour). The prompt-archive entries for panels 1, 2, 4 and 5 now store the current prompt text although their images date from 13 September; each carries a `revision_note` saying so. The article-illustration archive entry for Figure 1 (legacy key `06-incentives`) keeps its generation-time caption. The direct NBER page, the Academy of Management page and the full Holloway guide were not re-verified in this pass (the review’s gaps stand). Pre-existing uncommitted changes to chapters 04 and 05, `three-different-returns.md`, `valuation-is-an-estimate.md` and the other `_research` archives were left as found.

All `[[…]]` targets in index, summary and spec resolve and no literal `[[` remains in the built page; no “lose their jobs now”, “next two quarters”, “are obligations”, “larger share”, “hurdle” or “bridge” left in any format. ## 06-different-bets — Management Equity, Fund Carry and Employee Jobs Are Different Bets (`different-bets`)

**Files changed:** spec.md, index.md (`timetoread` 25 → 27 min), summary.md (500 words by the collection’s counter), comics.md (panel 4 caption and its stored prompt), `_research/comic-generation-prompts-20260913.json` (the `06-different-bets/04-scene` prompt, caption and `revision_note`), this log, the rebuilt `docs/private-techuity/different-bets.html` and `different-bets.spec.html`, and `manuscripts/owned/different-bets.md` with the export manifest. No image was changed: the review inspected all 11 images and raised no media finding, and the one caption edit does not touch anything the artwork shows.

- DB-011 (medium) — the €800,000 carry needed a stated distribution-stage assumption → **Fixed.** Step 4 of the payout arithmetic now states the assumption (every payment the fund agreement puts ahead of the manager has already been made, so each further euro of profit splits 80% to the fund’s investors and 20% to the manager) and shows 20% × €3.99 million = €798,000, about €800,000, with about €3.2 million to the fund’s investors. The following paragraph says a 20% rate does not by itself mean 20% of the next €4 million: a fund can be in profit and still short of the point where investors have their money and any preferred return back, in which case this money pays no carry; just past it a catch-up can give the manager more than 20% for a while; [[fund-economics]] works the stages. The four-bets table cell now reads “only once the fund has paid its investors everything its agreement puts first”. The summary keeps the amount with the same condition (“if the fund has already paid its investors everything its agreement puts first, so further profit splits 80/20. A profitable fund short of that point pays none yet”) and says the €100 million threshold has been passed.
- DB-008 (medium) — pause funding and the specialist fallback → **Fixed.** The specialists’ agreements, signed at the announcement, already cover a pause: the €120,000 is still paid at month twelve, and the same monthly rate (about €10,000 for the three together) is paid for each extra month they stay, up to three months. The board authorizes a pause contingency of up to €30,000 beside the €550,000 (most authorized: €580,000), and the finance leader runs the dated cash check on the longest authorized pause as well (three more months of payroll and premises, plus the €30,000). A new item separates continued payroll and premises (not new spending; the pause postpones €25,000 of saving a month) from the extra retention payments (new spending, from the contingency). “What goes back to the board”: a pause over three months, dropping a stage, new spending beyond €580,000, or a cash position in which the pause case can no longer be confirmed. “If a specialist leaves anyway”: the payment cannot require anyone to stay; the main-team engineer walked through that specialist’s customers takes them over with the other specialists’ help, the board is told, and paid cover beyond the approved amounts needs its approval. The three-month stage-two pause is worked through: gate passed at the end of month thirteen, the five leave and the location closes at month fifteen, full saving from month sixteen, €75,000 of saving postponed and €30,000 of retention payments added.
- DB-003 (medium) — summary vocabulary and the long closure paragraph → **Fixed.** Shares are “units of company ownership”; revenue is “money earned from sales”; the bonus counts “only profitable contracts, earning more in their first year than they cost to serve”; commission is “Salespeople’s commission, their pay for each sale”; “ten times yearly profit” replaces “earnings”; “alignment principles” became “Fund investors’ published principles on managers’ rewards”. The closure is now two paragraphs, the financial illustration and the staffing plan. Trimmed to hold the cap: the sentence that growth from some customers can hide the loss of others, “not ten”, and several connectives; no figure or condition was dropped.
- DB-012 (low) — dilution stated as unavoidable → **Fixed.** “An existing holder’s percentage falls unless that holder buys enough of the new shares to keep it”, with the 10-of-100 example (5% of 200 afterwards; buying 10 of the new shares keeps 10%); the percentage-versus-money distinction is kept.
- DB-013 (low) — “general partner” equated with the fund manager → **Fixed.** The bullet now says ILPA calls this the general partner’s commitment, and that in a fund set up as a partnership the general partner is the partner responsible for running the partnership, often a company affiliated with the fund manager rather than the manager itself (consistent with [[announcement-is-not-a-budget]] and the glossary).
- Terminology audit → taken: buyout (“buys enough of the company, often all of it, to control its decisions”), margin (“the part of each sale’s price that is left as profit”), the private-equity gloss in the Leslie–Oyer annotation (“funds that buy companies and hold them privately, with no shares traded on a stock exchange while the fund owns them”), and the comic’s panel 4 caption (“growth in revenue, the money earned from sales”). Not taken: spelling out ILPA inside the summary’s `[S02: …]` label, because the S-labels are uniform across the collection and the sentence before it now describes the source in plain words.

**Adapted:** DB-008 (the fallback is framed as a specialist resigning despite the signed extension, since the extension is agreed in advance and a payment cannot compel anyone to stay). DB-003 (profit is explained through “profitable contracts, earning more … than they cost to serve” rather than a separate definition sentence, to hold 500 words).
**Declined:** none.
**Unresolved / needs separate action:** the article is now about 5,400 prose words. The comic prompt-archive entries for panels 1, 2, 4 and 5 still postdate their images (noted in each `revision_note`); panel 4’s caption change does not affect its artwork, so nothing was regenerated. Four-column tables still scroll sideways at phone width (template behaviour). The direct NBER and Academy of Management pages and the full Holloway guide were not re-verified. Pre-existing uncommitted changes to other chapters and archives were left as found.
**Verification:** arithmetic recomputed by script (5% × €40m = €2m; 10/100 = 10%, 10/200 = 5%, 20/200 = 10%; €600,000 × 10 = €6m; 5% = €300,000; 70% × €5.7m = €3.99m; 20% = €798,000, 80% = €3.192m; 350 + 120 + 80 = 550, + 30 = 580; €120,000 ÷ 12 = €10,000; 3 × €10,000 = €30,000; 3 × €25,000 = €75,000; gate at end of month 10 + 3 = 13, + 2 months’ notice = month 15, saving from month 16; year-one figures unchanged). Summary counted at 500 words (figure lines, S-citation and markup stripped). `generate_comics.py --post 06-different-bets --dry-run` parses the six panels with nothing to generate. `python3 _wiring/build.py` built every journal; the built page contains the new passages, none of the superseded phrasings and no literal `[[`; manuscript export and `validate_manuscript.py` report `[valid]` (44 files, 110 images); `git status` has the same file set before and after the build and export. ## 06-different-bets — Management Equity, Fund Carry and Employee Jobs Are Different Bets (`different-bets`)

**Files changed:** spec.md, index.md (`timetoread` 27 → 28 min), summary.md (500 words by the collection’s counter), `_research/article-illustration-prompts-20260913.json` (only the `placement` string of the Figure 1 entry, which named the old section heading), this log, the rebuilt `docs/private-techuity/different-bets.html` and `different-bets.spec.html`, and `manuscripts/owned/different-bets.md` with the export manifest. comics.md and all 11 images are unchanged: the review inspected every image and raised no media finding, and no edited statement appears in the comic.

- DB-008 (medium) — the specialist fallback assumed a walkthrough that may not have happened → **Fixed.** “If a specialist leaves anyway” now covers only customers already walked through (the named main-team engineer takes over, with the other specialists’ help; Ines tells the board). Three new single-question items follow. “Customers not yet walked through” traces a month-two resignation and a month-five renewal: the support lead uses the weeks the specialist still works for walkthroughs of those customers, earliest renewal first; customers not reached pass to the two remaining specialists, who know the add-on but not that customer’s history; the specialists’ part in merging the two configuration systems is deferred, and any effect on the month-twelve date is reported at the next gate. “When that is not enough”: on a second departure, or if the two cannot cover the renewals falling due, Ines asks the board for paid cover; the departed specialist’s share of the €120,000 was approved as a retention payment and is not hers to redirect; she reconsiders the next stage at its gate without waiting for a trigger, and delaying stage one (within her three months) keeps the six colleagues on routine questions. “The gap that remains” says the departed specialist’s knowledge of a customer not yet walked through is gone, the stored configuration and the others’ knowledge are a partial substitute, a renewal may be lost, and trigger one is where it would show. The scarce-capacity item now says the six engineer-weeks pay for the walkthroughs only. No length is given for a resigning specialist’s notice, to avoid a new scenario assumption.
- DB-014 (medium) — summary statements stronger than the article → **Fixed.** “failing either withholds that quarter’s growth payment”; “Thirty customer contracts come up for renewal within six months”; “The board approves €550,000 of planned spending, €200,000 above the faster plan, plus up to €30,000 for a pause. - DB-015 (medium) — “ten times yearly profit” was not the article’s adjusted EBITDA → **Fixed**, with the review’s first option: “If each euro of lasting yearly saving adds €10 to the sale price, with debt and cash unchanged, shareholders’ value rises by about €6 million.” The summary no longer names an earnings measure; both formats apply €600,000 × 10.

# Revision log — in-depth review of Judge an Investor by Their Behavior Under Pressure, 18 September 2026

Response to round 1 of the in-depth review (`.in-depth-reviews/20260918-094055.I0JXHW/round-01.review.md`, findings IP-01 to IP-10 plus the terminology audit). The spec was updated before the article (seven new success criteria, `revised:` and a changelog line). Permalink and title unchanged. Every earlier scenario amount is unchanged (€5 million, €4.5 million, €1.5 million, €600,000, 65 hours, eight customers, fifteen months, ten operator days, week six, June/July/October). New fictional assumptions, all inside this chapter’s separate scenario: a thirty-day payment period after a valid request; the pilot result about four months after closing; a board decision on the second country around month eleven with payment due by month twelve; the €4.5 million pays for the pilot, the founder’s transition and the already approved plan, and none of it for the second country, which is budgeted at the €1.5 million; B’s stated answers about its fund (one of its last new investments, most remaining money earmarked for companies it owns, two one-year extensions expected, a likely sale within about five years). The shared scenario record does not mention this round and was not changed.

Explained at first use in the article: term sheet, shares, patient ownership, a missed plan (sales later than forecast, a product shipped late), quarter, CEO/CTO/CFO, governance, operating support, onboarding, implementation (about 80 hours today, the shared pilot’s baseline), pilot, release cycle, support platform, reference (and its reversed direction), investment thesis, engagement, operator, engagement charter, AI with a concrete example, where deal money goes, borrowed money and repayment dates, financial scenario, fund life, term, ILPA spelled out, reserve as an earmark, return, exit multiple, round, minority stake, valuation, bridge, operating partner, investment agreement, closing, dilution, shareholders’ agreement, lead investor, co-investor, operating cash, corporate investor, sales channel, downside scenario. “Benchmark conformity”, “use of proceeds”, “debt maturities”, “material”, “subscribe”, “uncalled commitments”, “callable”, “exit clock/pressure” were replaced by plain wording; Larkspur now “requests” the money, so the word “call” is no longer used at two levels. The summary and comic each define term sheet, fund, follow-on, pilot and onboarding on their own and avoid CFO, CTO and bridge. To Probe Further annotations now explain venture capital, VC, private equity, buyout, strategic versus financial corporate investment, and say “United States”.
- IP-02 (medium) — opening overstated what investors accept → **Fixed.** The callout presents an advance follow-on as something a company can ask for, binding only if the signed contract expressly says so, says not every investor can or will agree, and attributes the agreement to Investor B and the refusal to Investor A in this example.

The follow-on is split into “What B has set aside”, “What the contract would require” (a seven-row terms table: payer, amount, whether the fund can pay, when Larkspur may ask, other conditions, how long the right lasts, payment period) and “When Larkspur can spend” (three numbered states). The decision record is a six-item list (choice, alternatives rejected, risks accepted, scarce capacity, authorized by, evidence that would reopen it). The summary no longer reproduces the clause.

Other parts of a term sheet, such as confidentiality or a promise not to negotiate with other investors for a period, may bind the parties already.” Summary: “The term sheet here does not yet oblige B to invest; the signed agreement would.” No “binds nobody” remains in any format or built output.

No second-country hire, lease or customer promise precedes receipt, so nothing is spent ahead of the money. A timely request stays valid after the expiry date. The plan label in “When the Evidence Is Missing” and the chapter’s closing paragraph now say the €1.5 million is a right, not money, and hand only money actually received to Part III.

## 07-investor-under-pressure — Judge an Investor by Their Behavior Under Pressure (`investor-under-pressure`)

In-depth review round 3 (`round-03.review.md`, findings IP-13, IP-06, IP-14 and IP-15), 18 September 2026.

**Files changed:** spec.md (edited first: three criteria and a changelog line), index.md, summary.md (500 words by the same counter as rounds 1 and 2), this log, the rebuilt `docs/private-techuity/investor-under-pressure.html` and `.spec.html`, and `manuscripts/owned/investor-under-pressure.md`. No image, comic panel, caption, alt text or prompt archive changed: none of them shows the retest rule, the request window’s starting event, the opening callout or the comparison table.

- IP-13 (medium) — second pilot group assumed but not authorized → **Fixed.** The terms table says the group is “the one named in the pilot plan” and that “Larkspur and B agree that plan before signing and attach it to the agreement”. A new row, “If the first group misses”, gives the rule: one second group, allowed by the plan, so no new negotiation; at least eight new customers, taken in the order they signed so that nobody picks them; measured the same way; the second group’s own average decides and the two groups are not combined; waiting time and board confirmation apply unchanged; a third group would need B’s agreement. The timing paragraph expects the second result around month eight (this scenario’s assumption; the first result stays at month four, the decision at month eleven, payment by month twelve, expiry at month fifteen). A worked trace follows the review’s own test: first group 66 hours, second 62; the first misses, the second counts, and a valid request can be made at month eleven once the board confirms it and the other conditions hold; if the second also misses, no request can be made and the plan counts on no third group.
- IP-06 (low) — summary’s “first fifteen months” had no starting event → **Fixed.** The summary now says payment is due “within thirty days of a valid request. Larkspur may ask until fifteen months after closing, when the deal completes and money and shares change hands.” The closing line became “Once the first money arrives”, so “closes” is no longer used unexplained. Read alone with signing two months before closing, the window runs from closing, as in the article’s terms table, and the thirty days remain a separate payment period.
- IP-14 (low) — overloaded WHY INVESTORS CARE callout → **Fixed.** Cut from about 157 words to 77: writing conditions down gives up the freedom to refuse; not every investor can or will; one that does is bound only by the contract (conditions met, valid request made); one that won’t keeps the choice. Firm, fund, committee and follow-on are defined two paragraphs later, as before. B’s two reasons, A’s refusal and “one negotiated arrangement, not what investors generally accept” moved to “What the contract would require”, so no qualification was dropped.
- IP-15 (low) — comparison table: empty column heading, no row headers → **Partly fixed.** The first column is now headed “Aspect” in the site and the manuscript; the Investor A/B headings are unchanged. Semantic row headers are **not** added. The article’s tables are markdown; the shared renderer in `_templates/post.html` emits every body cell as `<td>`, and the manuscript exporter rejects raw HTML tables, so row headers need a change to the shared template (for example an opt-in `<div class="table-row-headers">` wrapper, handled like the existing `table-compact`, that turns first-column cells into `<th scope="row">`). That template is inlined into every page of all 21 journals (the last table change to it touched 631 built files), which is outside this round’s one-article scope.

**Also changed:** “capital” became “money” in the first closing question (terminology audit).
**Declined:** none. Left as they were from the audit, which requested no change: “profit” beside “sales”, “stock exchange”, “24/7”, “repeated mechanisms” in the article (the summary now says “patterns”), the abstract alt text of comic panel 4, the “TL;DR” tab label (shared site wording) and the small secondary labels drawn in comic panels 1 and 6.
**Unresolved / needs separate action:** row-header semantics for the comparison table (shared-template decision, above); no screen-reader pass was run. The four tables, the terms table now one row longer, still scroll sideways at phone width (template behaviour, with the visible cue and keyboard focus). The book PDF and EPUB downloads were not inspected. The review’s source gaps stand (full Chesbrough article, Venture Deals text, final Bernstein–Sheen article); no citation or URL changed.
**Verification:** by script: 496 ÷ 8 = 62 with two values above 65; 66 fails and 62 meets the 65-hour condition (the pooled 64 is not used); 4 < 8 < 11 ≤ 12 ≤ 15 months; averages of 59, 60, 62, 65 and 66 against both rules unchanged. ## 08-roadmap-to-revenue — The Chain From Roadmap to Revenue Breaks Easily (`roadmap-to-revenue`)

- R08-001 (medium) — terms before the example → **Fixed.** WHY INVESTORS CARE no longer uses “underwrite”, “dated conversion”, “valuation” or “repayment test”: it says what an investor is, that freed staff time counts only with a plan saying what the time is for and by when, and that the investor otherwise has nothing to use when estimating what the company is worth or checking whether cash covers loan payments. WHY YOU SHOULD CARE and the KEY POINTS use plain wording. Revenue, profit, margin (with a €20 on €100 example) and retention are defined in a four-item list before the first target is named; business case, implementation, board, product strategy, segments, hypothesis, billing, cash, pilot, capacity, financial year, payroll, cash forecast, bottleneck, operating budget, reserve and envelope are each explained at first use. DORA is expanded as DevOps Research and Assessment. The benefits table has plain row names and descriptions (“Shorten the time from a signed contract to a customer who is using the product and can be invoiced”, “Restore tests actually run, the failures the company has planned for…”, “Separate the part of the product that would have to change for a new country”). The later “Three terms” paragraph now defines only cash and financial contribution, so nothing is defined twice. The callouts stay first because that is the book’s fixed opening; the customer-setup example is still the first body paragraph.
- R08-002 (medium) — short formats not self-contained → **Fixed.** Summary rewritten with plain equivalents (larger share of sales kept as profit, customers staying, pay plus employment costs, first eight customers, work received, money held back, engineer-week defined) and the last block split into “The measured result”, “The spending” and “The next decision”; secondary ledger detail (maintenance start, quarterly review, expansion) dropped to hold 500 words; the figure caption now glosses the three abstract labels drawn in the overview image. Comic intro and captions 1, 2, 5 and 6 avoid margin, retention, cohort, reserve and “net benefit”; caption 5 explains “the whole intervention”, which is drawn in the bubble and on the proposal.
- R08-003 (medium) — missing orientation → **Fixed.** New short paragraphs “The example”, “The people” (Priya, Alex, Sam, Morgan, the board, day zero) and “Two kinds of work” (engineer-weeks versus staff hours per customer). The arithmetic is split into “The assumptions”, “The price of an hour” and “The sum”: 100 a year is a planning assumption, not the measured workload (the records cover twelve implementations in two quarters); the 80 hours count all Larkspur staff time on one setup, of which the specialist’s manual configuration is the part that holds the queue; 8,000 hours is four full-time people of modelled effort, not a staff count. The reserve is introduced with its opening balance (€200,000 and four engineer-weeks, inside the €500,000 / 24-week envelope) before the €160,000 / zero closing balance. “The specialist released 18 hours” became “the team spent 18 fewer hours on each setup”.
- R08-004 (medium) — cash wording → **Fixed.** “Cash and commitments at day 100” is now four labelled states: committed (€180,000), incurred (about €90,000, “Incurred does not mean paid… this scenario does not give the payment dates”), still to come (€90,000 over two quarters; maintenance from the operating budget) and cash benefit (none: the forecast carries the pilot’s payments but no benefit from released time). No payment amount is stated because the shared ledger has no payment schedule for the incurred half. Summary and comic caption 5 follow.
- R08-005 (medium) — non-cancellability inferred from an unexpired term → **Fixed, using the review’s second option.** The bullet reports only what the scenario holds: no contract spending had been cancelled by day 100; the dated saving in [[raise-what-you-need]] is not extending the contract after month twelve, if the queue is moving; at day 100 it was not, so the specialist was still needed; ending the work earlier would not by itself avoid a payment, because notice and payment terms are not set in this scenario. No cancellation term was invented.
- R08-006 (medium) — undefined waiting-time test, movable measurement boundary, “cheapest” → **Fixed.** Waiting time is defined (signed contract to first real scheduling), with a fictional baseline of about ten weeks in both groups. The month-six gate has two tests, both required: 50 hours or less counted end to end, including all staff time checking and correcting data before or during setup, with the bought-in validation and data-entry work reported beside the hours with its continuing cost; and eight weeks or less of waiting. The article states that 50 hours with waiting near ten weeks fails, and so does 50 hours reached by leaving data work uncounted. “Cheapest test” replaced by a comparison with the alternatives on the table (two hires at €300,000 a year; the full portal at €220,000 and twelve engineer-weeks the envelope no longer has). “Second stage” is explained as the next priced piece of onboarding work, needing its own approval, as in [[obligations-before-budget]].
- R08-007 (medium) — comic panel 1 artwork → **Fixed, image regenerated and inspected.** `generate_comics.py --post 08-roadmap-to-revenue --only 01-scene --overwrite`. The new panel shows a whiteboard headed “PROPOSED SETUP” with “Sign up → Shorter setup? → Start using product” and a large question mark, and a sheet headed “TODAY” with a longer row of unlabelled boxes, not crossed out. “FEWER STEPS, STEPS, IMMEDIATE ACCESS”, “DOC SUBMISSION”, the crossed-out waiting period and the “SETUP COMPLETE” tablet are gone. - R08-010 (low) — Magenta Book annotation → **Fixed.** “when assigning customers at random is impractical”; difference-in-differences is described as comparing the before-and-after change in the group that received the change with a group that did not; it “still needs a comparison group” and assumes similar earlier trends. One new fictional fact, recorded in `_research/shared-scenario-record.md`: the second engineer’s KNW-1 time (about four weeks alongside the specialist, from the operating team’s ordinary time, outside the 24).

## 28-cannot-fund-everything — You Cannot Fund Every Good Project at Once (`cannot-fund-everything`)

**Files changed:** spec.md (first), index.md (`timetoread` 10 → 16 min: 3,218 body words before the questions, excluding tables and figures, at the collection’s 200 wpm; the old text was 2,256 words, so the stated 10 was already low), summary.md (496 prose words; 632 with the two new tables’ cells), comics.md (intro, cast line, captions, alt text and stored prompts of panels 1, 2, 3, 4 and 6), regenerated `comic-01-scene.jpeg`, `comic-03-scene.jpeg` and `comic-06-scene.jpeg`, `_research/shared-scenario-record.md` (one added paragraph), `_research/comic-generation-prompts-20260913.json` (three entries, written by the generator), `_research/comic-generation-progress-20260913.json` (overwritten by the generator with this run’s result, as it always is), five superseded panel images archived by the generator under `_research/discarded-comic-variants/28-cannot-fund-everything-*`, this log, the rebuilt `docs/private-techuity/cannot-fund-everything.html`, `.spec.html` and the three panel copies, and `manuscripts/owned/cannot-fund-everything.md` with the manifest.

- R28-001 (high) — allocated confused with completed; “the last €40,000” → **Fixed.** The envelope is now defined as a limit on what may be committed in the hundred days, with committed, spent and finished kept apart. “All 24 engineer-weeks used” became “allocated” in the article and summary; “the last €40,000 and four weeks” became “another €40,000 and the last four unallocated engineer-weeks”, with €160,000 “still uncommitted”. A closing paragraph reconciles with 17: about ten of ONB-1’s twelve weeks used at day 100, about €90,000 of its €180,000 incurred (“incurred” defined), the other €90,000 due over the next two quarters, the data-quality step’s four weeks still to come. The summary carries a three-row committed / allocated / reserve table and the sentence “Committed is not spent, and allocated is not finished”.
- R28-002 (medium) — learner’s time missing from KNW-1 → **Fixed, with one new fictional fact.** The four protected weeks measure the specialist only; the second engineer spends about the same four weeks alongside, during the first 60 days: about eight person-weeks, all operating time, none from the 24, which were counted after both people’s KNW-1 time was set aside. Displaced work: non-urgent scheduling-engine changes, postponed by Alex; urgent fixes first. The table total is unchanged (“30 of the 24, plus 4 protected”) because the learner’s time is not envelope capacity; the decision record’s “Chosen” bullet names it. 17, 31 and the toolkit state only the specialist’s four weeks; they do not contradict this and were not edited.
- R28-003 (medium) — recovery requirement and retest scope → **Fixed.** Both parts of the requirement (dispatch back within four hours; no more than fifteen minutes of schedule updates lost) are stated before the table, in the table row, and in the summary and comic panel 2. The failure is described against both parts, including the unwritten step that applies the fifteen-minute backups; the correction’s three parts follow 11. The retest is qualified in all three formats: passed for loss of the application environment inside one cloud region; a regional outage untested and recorded as remaining risk. Also corrected against 11: the requirement was approved by Ines, not “agreed by the board”.
- R28-004 (medium) — dense terminology before the example → **Fixed.** “Why investors care” rewritten in plain words (no onboarding, pilot, engineer-weeks, reserve draw or slippage). Explained at first use: Larkspur, customer portal, board, closing, envelope, commit, diligence, chief executive officer (CEO), operating plan, dispatch, setup (= onboarding = implementation; implementation team and specialist), scheduling engine, release, growth funding, pilot, cohort, reserve, restore environment, backups, credential, database, incurred. “Constraint that will actually bind” → “which limit will be reached first”; “revenue” → “sales income”; “ledger” and “handover record” → plain descriptions.
- R28-005 (medium) — short formats not self-contained → **Fixed.** The summary and comic define an engineer-week, drop REC-1 / ONB-1 / KNW-1 for descriptive names, describe the training item, introduce Ines as chief executive officer and the board, and explain reserve and sunk cost with its decision implication. The summary’s two number paragraphs became two compact tables. The comic intro no longer says “fund scenarios”.
- R28-006 (medium) — panel 3 artwork → **Fixed in the image.** Regenerated with the journal’s `generate_comics.py`; inspected: “CASH: FITS” on a box of euro notes, a blue bar “24 WEEKS AVAILABLE”, a longer amber bar “30 WEEKS NEEDED” whose red hatched end reads “6 OVER”, and a separate green block “4 PROTECTED WEEKS: TRAINING”. Caption, alt text and prompt agree; 30 − 24 = 6. Bar lengths are illustrative, not to scale (the amber bar is drawn somewhat longer than 30:24).
- R28-007 (low) — panels 1 and 6 generic → **Fixed in the images.** Panel 1: four cards “SERVICE RESTORATION”, “SIMPLER SETUP”, “PORTAL BUILD”, “PORTAL RESEARCH” beside “CUSTOMERS WAITING”. Panel 6: “ORIGINAL PLAN” (restoration 4 weeks, setup 12, research 2, portal later) beside “AFTER FAILED TEST” (restoration 6 weeks, circled; the rest unchanged). Panel 6 took three generations: the first had Sam with shaved sides, the second drew Alex light-skinned and Sam as a child; the third has the right cast and lettering. Panels 2, 4 and 5 were inspected and kept; panels 2 and 4 got new captions and alt text that match their existing artwork.
- R28-008 (low) — Bezos annotation → **Fixed.** Type 1 = hard or impossible to reverse, Type 2 = reversible and so made faster; staging is named as this chapter’s application, not Bezos’s. “SEC” and “shareholders” are spelled out. The SEC URL was fetched (it redirects to the same document without the zero-padded company number; HTTP 200) and the filing index gives 5 April 2016, so “April 2016” stands.
- R28-009 (medium) — ONB-1’s continuing cost → **Fixed.** New “Continuing cost” bullet in the selection record: about €30,000 a year from the next financial year, paid from the operating budget, outside the €500,000 envelope and not part of the €280,000. The cash paragraph says the table’s amounts are one-time, the staging section points to it, and the summary carries one sentence. Agrees with 08’s “Who pays for what” table and 17.

**Also changed (terminology audit, no finding):** Reinertsen annotation glosses queues and cost of delay and no longer attributes the specialist example to the book; question 1 says “reached first”.
**Declined:** none.
**Unresolved / needs separate action:** the article grew by about 43% in body words (16 min). The summary’s prose is 496 words, but its two tables add 136 words of cells; if the collection’s counter includes table cells, the summary is over 500 and the tables would need to become prose. At 390 px both summary tables and the article table scroll sideways inside their frame (template behaviour, with the template’s hint); the first two columns are visible without scrolling. Panel 3’s bar labels are small at phone width; the caption carries the same numbers and the image links to full size. The header logo still shows faint generic “$” marks in a euro example (decorative; not a finding; not regenerated). 17, 31 and the toolkit do not state the second engineer’s KNW-1 time. The review’s source gaps stand for Reinertsen’s full text and the paid HBR and Arkes–Blumer articles. No screen-reader pass was run.
**Verification:** by script: 80 + 180 + 220 + 20 = 500; 4 + 12 + 12 + 2 = 30; 30 − 24 = 6; 280 / 18 → 220 / 6; 300 / 20 → 200 / 4; 340 / 24 → 160 / 0; 80 + 20 = 100; 4 + 2 = 6; 180 − 90 = 90; 4 + 4 = 8. All `[[…]]` targets in the four files resolve. Six comic JSON blocks parse; each caption sits inside its stored prompt and equals its visible text; each asset’s sha256 equals its `generation.sha256`; the `docs/` copies are byte-identical. No record code or CEO/CTO/CFO appears in the visible summary or comic text; nine stale phrases (“all 24 engineer-weeks used”, “the last €40,000”, “slippage”, “fund scenarios” and others) no longer occur. `python3 _wiring/build.py`: every journal built; against the run’s baseline status the only new changed files are this post’s sources, assets, generator records and built pages. A trial export to a temporary directory differed from `manuscripts/owned` in `cannot-fund-everything.md` only, so the real export did not disturb other chapters; `validate_manuscript.py` printed `[valid]` (44 files, 1,003 internal links, 110 images). Playwright (file://) at 1280 px and 390 px on the Article, TL;DR and Comic tabs: no page overflow, no console errors, no broken images, every image has alt text, no unresolved `[[…]]`, byline shows 16 min.

# Revision log — in-depth review, round 2, of You Cannot Fund Every Good Project at Once, 18 September 2026

Response to round 2 of the in-depth review (`.in-depth-reviews/20260918-141807.agAwJI/round-02.review.md`, findings R28-002, R28-005 and R28-010). The spec was updated before the article (three criteria tightened, changelog line). Permalink and title unchanged. No amount, date, threshold or scenario fact changed; the second engineer’s four or so KNW-1 weeks were already in `_research/shared-scenario-record.md` from round 1.

## 28-cannot-fund-everything — You Cannot Fund Every Good Project at Once (`cannot-fund-everything`)

**Files changed:** spec.md (first), index.md (`timetoread` 16 → 17 min: about 3,330 body words by round 1’s method, at 200 wpm), summary.md, comics.md (captions and stored prompts of panels 1, 3 and 6; alt text of panel 3), regenerated `comic-03-scene.jpeg`, `_research/comic-generation-prompts-20260913.json` (panel 3’s entry, written by the generator), `_research/comic-generation-progress-20260913.json` (overwritten by the generator with this run’s result, as it always is), the superseded panel archived as `_research/discarded-comic-variants/28-cannot-fund-everything-comic-03-scene-66ea0509a4de.jpeg`, this log, the rebuilt `docs/private-techuity/cannot-fund-everything.html`, `.spec.html` and the panel copy, and `manuscripts/owned/cannot-fund-everything.md` with the manifest.

- R28-002 (medium) — table and comic still showed only four protected training weeks → **Fixed, in the text and in the image.** The training row reads “About 8 protected person-weeks, outside the 24: 4 from the specialist and about 4 from the second engineer”; the total row reads “30 against the 24 available, plus about 8 protected outside the 24”. A sentence after the table says the eight or so weeks sit outside the 30-versus-24 comparison on both sides: set aside before the 24 were counted, so they neither add to the six-week shortfall nor can close it. The decision record’s “Chosen” and “Funding and scarce capacity” bullets carry the same figure, as does the summary (“about eight weeks, four from each person, set aside before the 24 were counted”). Comic panel 3 was regenerated with `generate_comics.py` and inspected: “CASH: FITS”, “24 WEEKS AVAILABLE”, “30 WEEKS NEEDED”, “6 OVER” and a separate green block labelled “ABOUT 8 PROTECTED WEEKS: TRAINING”; Sam matches the cast reference; one generation. Caption, alt text and stored prompt agree. €500,000, 30 versus 24 and the six-week shortfall are unchanged.
- R28-005 (low) — customer portal, cloud region and “managed service” → **Fixed.** Portal: the summary says it “would be an online workspace customers use themselves” (in the prose before the table, because a longer table cell pushed the number columns further off a 390 px screen); comic panel 1’s caption carries the same gloss. Cloud region: the article has a new short paragraph (Larkspur rents its computing from a cloud provider; a region is one geographic group of that provider’s computing facilities; the retest rebuilt Larkspur’s own systems inside a region that kept working; if the whole region were unavailable the service would have to come back somewhere else, untested and recorded as remaining risk), consistent with 11; the summary and comic panel 6 carry a two-sentence version. “Managed service” is replaced by a functional description: the backup credentials move out of one person’s account into a single, centrally controlled store for access credentials, which records who may use them. Panels 1 and 6 were **not** regenerated: their artwork contains none of the changed words, and the prompts archive keeps the prompts they were actually generated with.
- R28-010 (low) — summary over 500 words → **Fixed.** 497 words counted as rendered: prose plus every table heading and cell, numbers counted as words, image line and figure caption excluded (the rendered Summary pane’s full text, caption included, is 508 or fewer). Removed: the generic investor sentence in the opening, the prose restatement of the day-0 totals (€280,000 / 18 / €220,000 / six weeks now appear only in the decision table), the separate day-100 sentence (now the table’s third row), and both cross-links, whose expanded titles cost 17 words; the Article tab keeps them. Kept: the engineer-week definition, the training allocation, Ines proposes / the board approves / only the board draws on the reserve, the €30,000-a-year upkeep outside the limit, sunk cost, and the retest’s limitation. Both tables kept. The spec now says how the 300–500 words are counted.

**Declined:** none.
**Unresolved / needs separate action:** at 390 px the summary’s and article’s tables still scroll sideways inside their frame with the template’s hint (the first column takes about 317 px in the first summary table; template behaviour, not changed here). Panel 3’s bar lengths remain illustrative, and its labels are small at phone width; the caption carries the numbers and the image links to full size. 17, 31 and the toolkit still state only the specialist’s four KNW-1 weeks; nothing contradicts 28 and they were not edited. The review’s source gaps stand (Reinertsen’s full text; the paid HBR and Arkes–Blumer articles). No screen-reader pass was run. The all-journal build also refreshed `docs/private-techuity/different-bets.*` from another session’s in-progress edits to post 06; that is not part of this change.
**Verification:** by script: 80 + 180 + 220 + 20 = 500; 4 + 12 + 12 + 2 = 30; 30 − 24 = 6; 4 + 4 = 8; 280 / 18 → 220 / 6; 300 / 20 → 200 / 4; 340 / 24 → 160 / 0. All `[[…]]` targets in the four files resolve. Six comic JSON blocks parse; each caption sits inside its stored prompt and equals its visible text; each alt equals its image line; each asset’s sha256 equals its `generation.sha256`; the `docs/` copy of panel 3 is byte-identical. “managed service”, “4 protected” and “30 of the 24” no longer occur in the built page. `python3 _wiring/build.py`: every journal built. # Revision log — in-depth review, round 3, of You Cannot Fund Every Good Project at Once, 18 September 2026

Response to round 3 of the in-depth review (`.in-depth-reviews/20260918-141807.agAwJI/round-03.review.md`, findings R28-009, R28-011, R28-012 and R28-013). The spec was updated before the article (one criterion reworded, two added, one extended, changelog line). Permalink and title unchanged. No amount, date, threshold or scenario fact changed.

## 28-cannot-fund-everything — You Cannot Fund Every Good Project at Once (`cannot-fund-everything`)

**Files changed:** spec.md (first), index.md (`timetoread` stays 17 min: about 3,430 body words at 200 wpm), comics.md (caption and stored prompt of panels 4 and 6, and their visible text), this log, the rebuilt `docs/private-techuity/cannot-fund-everything.html` and `.spec.html`, and `manuscripts/owned/cannot-fund-everything.md` with the manifest. summary.md was not edited: it already says “Four proposals compete”, “A fifth item” and “30, against 24”. No image was regenerated.

- R28-011 (medium) — proposal table stretched by the no-wrap effort column → **Fixed in the post, not in the template.** Right-aligned cells are `white-space: nowrap` for every journal, so the cells were shortened instead: the training cell reads “(about 8)” and the total “30, against 24”, the same wording as the summary’s table. The trainer and learner breakdown moved to a short note directly beneath the table: the figure is in brackets because it is outside the 30-versus-24 comparison; about eight protected person-weeks, none of them from the 24, four from the specialist and about four from the second engineer; set aside before the 24 were counted, so they neither add to the shortfall nor close it. Measured in Playwright: at 1280 px the table is 712 px wide inside a 712 px pane with columns of 208 / 107 / 132 / 264 px (the effort column was wider than the pane before); at 390 px the table is the template’s 560 px minimum with columns of 147 / 102 / 131 / 179 px, so sideways scrolling no longer crosses a long empty column. A first attempt, “(about 8, outside the 24)”, still left a 195 px effort column on the phone and was shortened.
- R28-009 (low) — comic omitted the setup change’s continuing cost → **Fixed in the caption.** Panel 4’s caption, structured caption and stored prompt end with: “Approving the setup change also commits the company to its upkeep: about €30,000 a year from the next financial year, paid from the ordinary operating budget, outside the €500,000 limit.” The artwork (three selected cards, the full portal beside a question mark) carries no cost claim and was inspected in the review; it was not regenerated.
- R28-012 (low) — three, four and five requests → **Fixed.** The heading is “Competing Requests, Two Limits”. One sentence before the table says it has five rows: four proposals that need additional cash, and one training item that needs none, which is how the summary and comic already count them. The explanations that the portal research and build are stages of one idea, and that the training uses separately protected time, are kept. The historical entry in this log that names the old heading was left as written.
- R28-013 (low) — dense capacity bullet and panel 6 caption → **Fixed.** The day-0 “Funding and scarce capacity” bullet is three sentences: the allocation (€280,000 of €500,000, 18 of 24), the implementation specialist’s twelve consecutive weeks, and KNW-1’s protected time outside the 24 (four weeks and four or so). Panel 6’s caption is three short paragraphs: the failure (eleven hours against four, a night of updates lost, €80,000 and four weeks spent and unrecoverable); the approval (€20,000 and two more weeks from the reserve, for a correction and a retest by day 85) followed by a separate sentence for the totals (€300,000 and 20 committed, €200,000 and four weeks in reserve, portal deferred); and the retest’s scope (one kind of failure, inside a cloud region that kept working, the region defined, whole-region loss untested). The structured caption and stored prompt hold the same text with `\n\n` between paragraphs, which satisfies `generate_comics.py`’s caption-in-prompt and caption-in-text assertions. Panel 6’s artwork is unchanged.

**Declined:** none. The terminology audit requested no other change.
**Unresolved / needs separate action:** the prompts archive keeps the prompts panels 4 and 6 were actually generated with, so their archived captions are earlier ones (as after rounds 1 and 2). At 390 px the article and summary tables still scroll sideways inside their frame with the template’s hint (560 px minimum width; template behaviour, not changed here).
